CASS4 Gene Directs Microglia to Contain Alzheimer’s Plaques

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Neuroscientists at the University of Virginia School of Medicine have uncovered the elusive biological mechanism of CASS4, a prominent genetic risk factor for Alzheimer’s disease. In a study published in the Journal of Experimental Medicine, researchers led by Dr. John Lukens demonstrated that CASS4 acts not within neurons as previously assumed, but directly within microglia, the brain’s resident immune cells.

The gene functions as a master regulator enabling microglia to surround, contain, and tightly compact toxic amyloid-beta plaques into manageable clusters. When CASS4 activity is impaired, microglia fail to envelop plaques, allowing neurotoxic proteins to spread unrestrained, triggering synaptic destruction, neuronal death, and rapid cognitive decline.

Source: University of Virginia / Journal of Experimental Medicine

More than six million Americans live with Alzheimer’s disease, a progressive neurodegenerative disorder that erodes memory, executive function, and personal identity. Ranked as the sixth leading cause of death in the United States, Alzheimer’s is characterized pathologically by two hallmark protein aggregations: extracellular amyloid-beta plaques and intraneuronal neurofibrillary tangles composed of hyperphosphorylated tau.

Large-scale genome-wide association studies (GWAS) have repeatedly flagged CASS4 (Cas Scaffold Protein Family Member 4) as a significant genetic risk locus for late-onset Alzheimer’s disease. However, despite its consistent link to dementia risk, its molecular function and cellular site of action inside the brain remained unknown.

Now, a research team at the University of Virginia School of Medicine led by Dr. John Lukens has resolved this question.

Publishing in the Journal of Experimental Medicine, the investigators reveal that CASS4 operates as a critical genetic coordinator of microglia, the central nervous system’s specialized immune scavengers. Rather than serving an intrinsic neuronal role, CASS4 orchestrates the microglial machinery required to corral and compact neurotoxic debris, preventing widespread collateral damage to neighboring circuits.

Under healthy physiological conditions, microglia constantly survey brain parenchyma with motile branches. When toxic amyloid-beta monomers oligomerize into fibrillar deposits, healthy microglia migrate toward the site, forming a dense protective barrier around the plaque core.

This microglial wall physically isolates the toxic fibrils and mechanically compacts them into dense, tightly packaged bundles, preventing neurotoxic oligomers from diffusing outward and poisoning adjacent neuronal synapses.

When Lukens’ team disrupted CASS4 function in preclinical models, this defense collapsed:

“These findings break new ground in our understanding of how this genetic risk factor contributes to Alzheimer’s disease,” said Dr. Lukens, director of UVA’s Harrison Family Translational Research Center in Alzheimer’s and Neurodegenerative Diseases. “Our work further suggests that defective CASS4 activity may serve as an early-onset biomarker to detect Alzheimer’s and that therapeutic strategies which boost its function may offer a much-needed approach to treat this devastating brain disease.”

Historically, neurodegenerative drug development focused heavily on targeting neurons directly or attempting to chemically dissolve amyloid aggregates after they formed. The UVA team’s findings provide evidence for neuroimmunological modulation as an alternative strategy.

“Despite CASS4 being an Alzheimer’s risk gene, really nothing is known about what this gene does or how it affects the brain during disease. Our lab sought to address this major knowledge gap with this paper,” explained lead author Addie Walsh, a UVA physiology graduate student who conducted the foundational experiments before heading to Novartis’ Neurodegeneration Team to continue microglial research. “These are important findings because they give us new insight into how this understudied AD risk gene functions in the brain and suggest that CASS4 may normally help microglia respond to amyloid pathology.”

The researchers demonstrated that the loss of CASS4 causes broad downstream dysregulation of microglial transcriptional networks, influencing cellular clearance mechanisms relevant to multiple protein-misfolding disorders.

“Many in the field initially presumed that CASS4 was likely functioning in neurons to influence Alzheimer’s. Our findings shift this paradigm by proving that CASS4 predominantly acts through the immune system,” Lukens noted. “This is particularly exciting, as it has only been in recent years that immune-targeting strategies have been considered for the treatment of neurodegenerative diseases, and this remains an untapped therapeutic avenue with tremendous potential.”

Lukens’ laboratory, affiliated with both the Harrison Family Translational Research Center and UVA’s Center for Brain Immunology and Glia (BIG Center), is now leveraging resources within the newly launched Paul and Diane Manning Institute of Biotechnology. The institute is specifically engineered to translate foundational biomedical discoveries into clinically actionable therapeutics.

The investigators are working to screen small-molecule agonists and gene-therapy vectors capable of upregulating CASS4 expression or mimicking its scaffolding activity within human microglia.

By restoring the brain’s endogenous capacity to compact and neutralize metabolic toxins, future immunotherapies targeting the CASS4 pathway could offer a targeted strategy to halt Alzheimer’s progression before extensive neuronal loss occurs.

What is the CASS4 gene, and how does it relate to Alzheimer’s disease?

CASS4 is a genetic risk factor repeatedly identified in genome-wide association studies for Alzheimer’s disease. This study reveals that CASS4 directs microglia to act as the brain’s “trash compactors,” forming protective physical barriers around toxic amyloid-beta plaques to minimize neurotoxic exposure.

What happens to the brain when CASS4 function is disrupted?

When CASS4 is inactive or impaired, microglia fail to surround and compress amyloid-beta plaques. As a result, the plaques grow faster, spread more aggressively into surrounding tissue, damage nearby neurons, and accelerate memory loss and cognitive decline.

Why does this study change how scientists view Alzheimer’s treatments?

Most previous research assumed CASS4 operated within neurons. Proving that it acts through microglia confirms that immune dysfunction is central to Alzheimer’s pathology, opening opportunities for therapies designed to boost microglial clearance rather than solely targeting neuronal pathways.

Alzheimer’s genetic risk factor CASS4 controls microglial focal adhesion biology and amyloidosis

Recent genome-wide association studies have identified numerous Alzheimer’s disease (AD) risk genes enriched in microglia, including Crk-associated substrate (Cas) scaffold protein family member 4 (CASS4), which encodes the adaptor protein CASS4. Despite its strong genetic association with AD, the role of CASS4 in disease remains poorly understood.

Here, we show that full-body Cass4 deletion in 5xFAD mice increases amyloid-β (Aβ) deposition, neuronal dystrophy, and cognitive deficits. Since microglia are the predominant brain-resident cells expressing Cass4, we investigated its role in microglial responses to Aβ pathology. Loss of CASS4 impaired microglial mobilization to Aβ plaques and reduced plaque compaction.

Phosphoproteomic analysis of purified microglia revealed marked disruption of focal adhesion signaling following Cass4 deletion. Consistent with this, CASS4 deficiency abrogated in vivo microglial recruitment to injury and in vitro wound healing responses, two processes that are centrally orchestrated by focal adhesion biology.

Together, these findings identify CASS4 as a critical regulator of microglial function and focal adhesion biology and suggest that CASS4 may represent a therapeutic target for AD.

Thune will lead Republicans through the minority

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Senate Majority Leader John Thune was reelected as the Republican leader on Tuesday and will steer the conference through their time in the minority.

The South Dakotan ran uncontested despite a push from some of his colleagues to delay the leadership elections following Republicans’ stunning defeats last week in the midterms, which saw them lose at least seven seats and the majority.

“Obviously, the election result was not what we hoped for,” Thune told reporters, referring to the midterms. “But I think it’s important that we take the lessons from that and internalize the message the American people were sending to us.”

While he and the rest of the leadership slate sailed through the conference contests by voice vote, it wasn’t without drama.

Before the leadership elections began in the Old Supreme Court Chamber early Tuesday morning, the conference held a meeting at the request of a group of GOP five senators, led by Sen. Ron Johnson, R-Wis.

The conversation, which several senators described as “frank,” ended up pushing back the leadership elections by a few hours. But they were not postponed to another day as some had hoped.

“This has never been anything against John Thune,” said Johnson, as he made good on his commitment to leave the room early if the conference proceeded with its election. “What I didn’t agree with was holding the election right away, giving nobody an opportunity to really challenge any leadership position.”

“Part of the issue here is who wants the job? God bless him for serving,” Johnson added.

The conference conversation Tuesday morning turned into an “open-mic” with both conservatives and those who lost their elections speaking, according to senators.

Sen. Josh Hawley, R-Mo., told reporters, “To rush into this and to just ratify all the same leadership — I mean, what message does that send?”

But no contender to Thune emerged despite the angst, and a motion to proceed to the election, introduced by Thune’s fellow South Dakotan Sen. Mike Rounds, was accepted.

Sen. Rand Paul, R-Ky., said voters who rejected Republicans last week were reacting to high gas prices, the war in Iran and the cost of living, not to how Senate leaders were running the conference.

“I don’t think anybody in Texas who voted for the Democrat governor was voting against John Thune. If you diagnose that as a problem, you’re misdiagnosing the problem,” Paul said.

Whip John Barrasso, R-Wyo., was reelected to his position, as was the chairman of the Senate Republican Conference, Tom Cotton of Arkansas. Sen. James Lankford of Oklahoma was again selected as vice chair of the conference, while Sen. Shelley Moore Capito of West Virginia was reelected as Republican Policy Committee chair.

“Our first step is to make sure the American people know that we heard what they said, and the American people’s priorities are our priorities. That’s the way we regain their trust,” Barrasso said afterward.

Sen. Bernie Moreno of Ohio got the nod to chair the National Republican Senatorial Committee, meaning he will helm the campaign arm as the party looks ahead to the uncertain waters of 2028.

Moreno said, “I was in the retail automobile business, so when you don’t listen to your customers, your customers punish you. I think we have to own it, and I think we have to learn from it.”

Sen. John Kennedy, R-La., said on Monday evening, “What we as a conference right now need is honesty. We need focus. We need to try to reach a consensus on what we need to change.”

Kennedy was among those pushing to delay the leadership elections and told reporters on Tuesday he voted present instead of yes on whether to proceed to them.

Sen. Rick Scott, R-Fla., who ran for GOP leader twice — once in 2022 against Sen. Mitch McConnell, R-Ky., and in 2024 against Thune — declined to run again to challenge Thune. But in a Dear Colleague letter on Monday, he detailed what he’d like to see in the lame-duck session before Democrats take power, including confirming every nominee President Donald Trump has sent to the Senate, funding the government without an omnibus, banning stock trading, passing the permitting bill that is advancing this week in the Senate, passing the National Defense Authorization Act, balancing the budget and pushing through a sweeping voter ID bill known as the SAVE America Act.

Senate Republicans on Tuesday also voted on a resolution on their path forward that was spurred by a motion from Scott, aides said.

“It is the sense of the GOP Conference that we will maximize the 54 days we have with congressional majorities, to confirm every judicial nominee and every executive nominee that the conference approves of, to pass our core Republican priorities, and to do everything humanly possible to pass a reconciliation bill before Jan. 3,” the resolution reads in part.

That long list of goals highlights the expectations Thune has faced as leader, where he led one-third of Republicans’ trifecta in Washington. In the next Congress, Democrats will hold the majority in both chambers of Congress after their larger-than-expected wins, while Trump finishes out his term as president.

Thune said he told his conference: “As I look back on the last couple of years, my question is, what would you have me do differently?”

“Given the constraints we had to deal with, all the stuff that was coming at us on a daily basis, a narrow margin in the House, the White House, the Democrats over here who were obstructing two long government shutdowns — I think we came out of it as well as could have been expected, and got a lot of stuff done,” Thune told reporters.

“If you believe the exit polls, it was the war, it was the economy, it was inflation, it was fuel prices,” Thune added. “So no, I wasn’t apologizing for anything that we did. And I think on the political side, I don’t know what else I could have done.”

Man Utd Make Jason Wilcox and Omar Berrada Sack Decision

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Manchester United are believed to have already reached their verdict not just on the future of Michael Carrick, but also regarding two other key higher-ups, Omar Berrada and Jason Wilcox, who have also received serious scrutiny over recent months.

With multiple outlets now reporting that the former Man Utd midfielder turned interim boss and now full-time head coach is set to be relieved of his duties in the very near future – and with United's preferred candidate to replace Michael Carrick already named – pundits, supporters, and football fans in general are debating where exactly the book stops at the giant of a sporting brand, as countless have questioned where the true accountability for their poor fortunes in the post-Fergie era lies.

Berrada and Wilcox, both of whom found success on the business side of things during their time at Man City, are now the latest to be targeted by chants around Old Trafford and protest banners hanging from stands in the Stretford End.

Manchester United chiefs are expected to sack Michael Carrick

Nevertheless, while they're still trying to back their manager and thank him for his efforts during one of the most difficult periods in the club's long history, GIVEMESPORT understands that the pair are safe. At least for now, anyway.

As per The Telegraph's James Ducker and other sources, the executive duo — Berrada serving as CEO and Wilcox as Director of Football, respectively — are expected to keep their jobs if/when Carrick is dismissed; it's said they maintain co-owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe's support as part of a core triumvirate.

Since INEOS' investment in Manchester United, they have already shuffled significantly not only when it comes to the backroom staff but within the boardroom too, but it looks as though he still considers them key advisers and pillars of the so-called 'Project 2028' rebuild.

Ratcliffe has reiterated that, despite stating that he doesn't mind being "unpopular" and making the tough decisions, the increasing levels of abuse he and his cohort have received over the course of their tenure at the club, stating that he would walk away from the whole franchise if it ever became severe and prolonged as the anti-ownership sentiment and demonstrations the Glazer family have seen.

On the one hand, Wilcox's involvement in footballing decisions has been perceived as an issue by many Reds (this being the ex-player's first role of this kind), with divisive defender Lisandro Martínez publicly criticising the sporting director's decision-making in his highly inflammatory post-match quotes following their Champions League defeat to Atlético Madrid.

Meanwhile, more senior 'leadership' figures such as the World Cup winner himself have equally come under fire for sub-par performances of late, making key match-deciding errors and, crucially, irrespective of whatever big statements made in high-profile interviews, not doing enough talking on the pitch.

For most United match-goers and fans who watch it with their eyes week in, week out, whether or not Carrick stays or goes won't be enough to fix the deep-seated problems running throughout Manchester United; they'd much prefer wholesale change from the very top of the hierarchy all the way down - though it's likely they'll be waiting a long time to see that on a big enough scale to quell their frustrations.

'Green gold until the club is sold', as the supporters' saying goes, but do any of you really foresee the Glazers selling up one of the biggest sporting franchises in the world anytime soon?

Le rugby de retour dans le Chaudron ?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Vendredi dernier, le président de la Fédération française de rugby, Florian Grill était à Saint-Etienne pour inaugurer, à l'Etivallière, le terrain de rugby Jean-Michel Lionard (Responsable des sports à la Ville de Saint-Etienne sous le mandat de Michel Thiollière), terrain qui vient juste d'être rénové.

Florian Grill n'avait pas apprécié que le précédent maire, Gaël Perdriau l'accuse publiquement l'an dernier de ne pas tenir ses promesses, notamment pour la rénovation de ce terrain Lionard.

C'était donc l'occasion d'apaiser les relations entre les deux parties et de voir si le stade Geoffroy-Guichard pourrait prochainement recevoir des rencontres de rugby. La ville de Saint-Etienne est en effet candidate pour accueillir des matchs de l'équipe de France féminine, des U20 et du rugby à 7.

How to get the crude lockpick recipe in DREADMOOR, all lockpick types, and how to use them

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Although brute force can take you far in DREADMOOR, having a lockpick at hand is a requirement if you wish to get past locked doors and access treasure chests across the game’s many settlements.

But getting the tool means first acquiring its recipe, something only doable a few hours in. Here’s how to get the first lockpick recipe in DREADMOOR and how the mechanic works.

You can unlock the Crude Lockpick recipe in DREADMOOR as a reward by completing the Catch a Mutant Fish Story Quest. The quest is unlocked after meeting Mad Nick on his island and requires catching two Cryptmaw fish.

The fish can be found in the locations below, but only late at night. You will also need to have at least 30 Line Strength to attempt the catch, so don’t forget to equip the Braided Line to your fishing rod at the trading post to meet the requirements.

After returning to Mad Nick and unlocking the recipe, you will be able to craft the Crude Lockpick by using 1x Rubber and 1x Metal Plate. You can have a maximum of twelve in your inventory. Just don’t forget, they will share slots with First Aid Kits.

Read what’s behind the latest news in gaming (with a bit of snark) by subscribing to the Destructoid Checkpoint newsletter!

You can pick locks in DREADMOOR by first using your mouse to position your lockpick and find the sweet spot within the lock, marked by a glowing light. Now, hold D to turn the lock while keeping the tool in the right position to unlock it. Forcing the lock will cause your lockpick to break.

Apart from the Crude Lockpick, you can unlock two other variants in DREADMOOR: the standard iron Lockpick and the Master Lockpick. Both are clear upgrades and can be crafted after unlocking their respective recipes.

We will be adding more to the above once we unlock the recipes for the advanced lockpicks. In the meantime, don’t forget to check out all Fishing Rods and how they work in DREADMOOR.

He joined the Destructoid team in May 2024 as a Contributing Writer and can always be found either thinking about Final Fantasy VII or eagerly waiting for news on Kingdom Hearts IV.

Model Caroline Trentini Reflects of Her Much-Memed Shoot With Iowa’s Governor-to-Be Rob Sand and Steven Meisel

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

One day in the early 2000s at Pier 59 Studios, Steven Meisel gathered a cast of young, up-and-coming models that included Lisa Cant, Diana Dondoe, Caroline Trentini, and Iowa native and then-college student Rob Sand. Brana Wolf styled, Garren was on hair, and Dame Pat McGrath painted the makeup. Grungy styles from Helmut Lang, Prada, and Gucci were on the racks. Titled “Be My Baby,” the shoot explored the renaissance of punkish adolescence on the runways, and while the spread originally appeared in the February 2004 pages of Vogue Italia, over two decades later, the shoot’s legacy has taken on a completely new life.

Last week, we connected the dots that Iowa Governor-Elect Rob Sand was once a high fashion model, walking the runways for brands including Prada and Japanese cult label Number Nine (a recent photo has since circulated of him sporting a sweatshirt from the show that he walked in 2004). Following our post, he took to Instagram with images from “Be My Baby,” with the caption, “I’m disappointed to learn images of me modeling for Prada are circulating. Because this shot from Italian Vogue is much better.”

Steven Meisel photographed the shoot in question. And not long after Sand’s post, the legendary lensman shared the 2004 image of Sand and Trentini to his own feed. We went directly to Trentini to hear her side of the story.

“It was a huge surprise,” she said. “Honestly, every moment I’ve ever spent with Steven Meisel feels like it happened yesterday. I remember everything, the smells, the atmosphere, the music,” she continued. “This was my very first Steven shoot, and I was very aware of the importance of that day.” In many ways, she and Sand were both just kids. “This particular photo was taken very quickly, but it’s also one of my favorites from the shoot. Rob really made the difference because even though we were both a bit nervous and shy, he was really immersed in the story, just as I was.”

Trentini recalls the experience as a defining moment that shaped the rest of her career—any model’s first shoot with Meisel was and is a breakthrough achievement, though Sand’s accomplishment as Iowa’s next governor likely takes the cake for his chosen path. “[Meisel] taught me everything about my attitude, how to carry myself, and how to find my best angle when the camera clicked,” said Trentini. As for the future of her former castmate, “Rob has wonderful values that I share,” she said. “Plus, he might just be the first governor ever to have had the honor of posing for Steven Meisel!”

Popular Thanksgiving Product Recalled From Costco Shelves Over Concerning Contamination Risk

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Here's what shoppers need to know ahead of the holiday.

Delish editors handpick every product we feature. We may earn commission from the links on this page.

Costco members, listen up. A popular Thanksgiving topping is being recalled over concerns about possible cleaning chemical contamination. Here's what to watch for.

Costco is recalling its Kirkland Signature Real Heavy Whipped Cream ahead of the upcoming Thanksgiving holiday. The recall covers 15-ounce cans sold between October 11 and October 31, 2026, including those purchased in three-packs.

To identify the affected product, check for item number 1777000, item code AC50, and a best-if-used-by date of April 2, 2027. The cans must also have a printed timestamp between 00:35 and 02:45.

The whipped cream was sold at Costco warehouses in 12 states: Alabama, Florida, Georgia, Iowa, Illinois, Indiana, Missouri, Mississippi, North Carolina, South Carolina, Tennessee, and Wisconsin.

Producer Alamance Foods voluntarily recalled the product after learning that one batch may contain a small amount of a cleaning chemical. According to a November 7 letter sent to Costco members, some shoppers reported a chemical smell and taste.

The company said no serious adverse health effects requiring medical care had been reported. The notice does not identify the chemical or say how it may have gotten into the whipped cream.

If your can matches the affected lot and timestamp, stop using it and return it to your local Costco for a full refund. The company's notice also includes photographs of the can and its printed code to help shoppers identify the product.

For questions or concerns, contact Alamance Foods at customer.care@alamancefood.com.

DC Studios Boss Jeremy Latcham Wants More Family-Friendly Movies In Hollywood

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Remember when nobody cared who was in charge at movie studios and we just watched the actual films? Those days are long gone, as evidenced by the latest debacle at Skydance, where DC Studios co-heads James Gunn and Peter Safran are out. The pair are set to be replaced by longtime Marvel Studios producer and Kevin Feige colleague, Jeremy Latcham,who has a more wholesome, family-friendly sensibility than his predecessors. In fact, he wants more family entertainment from Hollywood in general.

At this point, it remains to be seen just what the Marvel Studios alum, who will officially become president and chief creative officer of DC Studios on December 1, 2026, has in store for DC, or indeed how much creative control he'll have. What we do know about Latcham is that he's all for family-friendly fare. He previously spoke to The Christian Broadcasting Network in an interview entitled "Hollywood Producer Behind Massive Marvel Hits Loves Jesus."

The new face of DC we are cooked. RIP Peacemaker and anything rated R pic.twitter.com/W50CYpfBeO

In this interview from June 2026, Latcham refers to the "Grandma Test" he used during his Marvel tenure "When we were making the first Marvel movies, I'd always be like, 'I don't know if my grandma would like that, you know?'" he said, adding:

"And that became like a guiding light of like as we were developing the movies, I just had this sense of, 'I don't know if my grandma would like that,' and that became the stand-in [to determine if a movie] is going to work for regular people that are outside of the bubble of Hollywood. What does this [movie] say for a regular family?"

While we ponder how DC's cinematic future could be indirectly influenced by Jeremy Latcham's nana, it's worth noting that this "grandma test" is just one expression of Latcham's fondness for wholesome entertainment.

Jeremy Latcham has made no secret of his affinity for family-friendly movies that put heart and characters at the forefront. In another clip making the rounds on Twitter/X, the newly-appointed DC Studios head spoke about how studios have forsaken the most important elements of film in order to churn out empty spectacle.

The new DC Studios boss, Jeremy Latcham, talks about the importance of storytelling, making movies that connect with audiences, and creating memorable cinematic experiences.#DCU #DCComics #DCStudios pic.twitter.com/Wnnancri19

— Phil The Assyrian King (@PhilTheAssyrian) November 10, 2026

"The thing that I learned most at Marvel, and the thing that drives the movie, and it's something that I feel very strongly about, is heart, humor, and spectacle, in that order," Latcham said, before adding that most people in Hollywood get it wrong. "A lot of people in town say, 'We're making a spectacle film and the characters don't matter as much and the humor doesn't matter as much because it's all about the spectacle.'" According to him, however, spectacle is readily available in our society. What matters is heart and "characters that you can actually invest in." Doesn't seem like a bad point, does it?

Unfortunately, that perfectly reasonable assessment of the modern day movie industry recently led Latcham to produce one of the most forgettable movies of 2026 in "The Breadwinner," the family-oriented comedy starring Nate Bargatze that earned a 19% critic score on Rotten Tomatoes. Otherwise, besides producing big Marvel hits such as "Iron Man," "Spider-Man: Homecoming," and, funnily enough, James Gunn's "Guardians of the Galaxy," Latcham has been involved with "Dungeons & Dragons: Honor Among Thieves" and "Bad Times at the El Royale." But "The Breadwinner" was most obviously aligned with Latcham's stated sensibilities, and that isn't the most reassuring fact.

Jeremy Latcham left Marvel Studios in 2017 and between producing mainstream movies has also been involved in several Christian projects, including the Biblical TV series "House of David." He previously appeared on the Bible Bros podcast where he talked about using his faith during his time at Marvel Studios. "It wasn't like instilling, like, faith per se," he said. "But it was like instilling redemptive qualities into these characters and seeing the growth that they could have." Latcham doesn't seem interested in injecting overt Christianity into DC's output. But his fondness for family-friendly entertainment obviously has a Christian basis.

The new DC Studios head went on to say, "There's not been a theatrical family comedy [...] in the theaters in, like, over a decade. They just abandoned it [...] And I was like, 'I think people will go see this movie if you make it.'" Sadly, "The Breadwinner" didn't prove his point. But clearly this guy wants more movies to be a lot more wholesome than they currently are.

Whether he'll use DC to try to shift things in that direction remains unclear. A new report claims that Skydance CEO David Ellison and his cohort plan on dismantling James Gunn and Peter Safran's DC Universe, clearing the way for a new take on the source material. But it's unlikely Latcham will have much creative freedom. Ellison, who runs the newly-merged Paramount and Warner Bros., is having Latcham report to Skydance studio heads Dana Goldberg and Josh Greenstein rather than directly to him. As such, Latcham will have an extra level of oversight. Whether he'll actually get any closer to his vision of a family-friendly, PG-13 Hollywood utopia at DC is yet to be determined.

Oura is acquiring medical AI chatbot startup Counsel Health

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Posts from this topic will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Doctor visits could start with a readout of your Oura ring data.﻿

Doctor visits could start with a readout of your Oura ring data.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Oura announced its quarterly earnings today and said it agreed to acquire Counsel Health for $625 million, after adding the company’s AI chatbot to its app earlier this year. Counsel Health calls itself an “AI-native primary care company,” using the app to deliver AI-generated responses and insights based on users’ health data reported from their wearables that can then proceed to a conversation with one of its in-house physicians.

Oura says it expects the deal to close before the end of the year.

Oura CEO Tom Hale said in an interview with Bloomberg that the acquisition is part of a plan to connect wearables, clinical AI, and access to doctors based on insights from biometric data to answer questions or monitor ongoing conditions. Counsel Health’s AI chatbot can connect Oura users to a real doctor directly from the app and draw on users’ biometric data from their wearables in its answers to medical questions. The plan they describe in the press release is to focus on three areas, including preventative care, acute care for episodic health concerns, and specialty and chronic care for things like reproductive and cardiometabolic health.

According to Bloomberg, Counsel Health’s CEO Muthu Alagappan says it’s also planning to expand its network of on-demand physicians, which is currently “made up of about 50 people.” Counsel’s 70 employees will join Oura as part of the deal, with Alagappan becoming Oura’s chief clinical AI officer. Oura says in its announcement that Counsel will also continue offering its medical AI platform outside of Oura’s user base through new and existing enterprise partners, including “health plans, health systems, consumer health organizations, and employers.”

Brenda Song Says She Felt “Underpaid and Overworked” on Disney’s ‘The Suite Life’ Shows

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

"I feel like there are parts of the 'Suite Life' that are, like, black holes for me. Like, I don’t remember years of my life," Song said while speaking with her former co-star, Dylan Sprouse.

Brenda Song is reflecting on her time as a child actor, admitting that she felt “underpaid and overworked” while working on Disney‘s The Suite Life shows.

The Running Point star appeared on her former co-star Dylan Sprouse‘s Wildmen podcast, where they recalled working on The Suite Life of Zack & Cody franchise for Disney, and its subsequent spinoff The Suite Life on Deck.

“I’ve been curious to ask you about this,” Song said to Sprouse. “I feel like there are parts of the Suite Life that are, like, black holes for me. Like, I don’t remember years of my life.” Related Stories TV 'Frozen' Is Getting the 'A Real Bug's Life' Treatment With Nat Geo, Disney+ and Hulu's 'The Real Frozen' Movies Watch Disney's Holiday Short 'The Invisible Horse' From 'Paddington in Peru' Director Dougal Wilson

Wildmen co-host Brendan Columbus recounted a recent episode of the podcast they did with Phill Lewis, noting, “Phill said he forgot directing episodes. It was like, he’s like, ‘They’re just gone.’ He’ll watch them again. Just have no recollection.”

Song said looking back on her time on the Disney Channel series, it felt like “we paid them.” She added, “I always say this about my time at Disney, I know everybody has their own journey… I always say, like, I have a boring tale. I didn’t have this crazy, like, ‘Oh my God, this was so — I had a lovely time.”

The actress admitted that “the only thing that was hard about that time was I feel like we were underpaid and overworked.” Song added, “But Disney did give us a platform to be sitting here right now, so it’s very hard to complain about certain things.”

The Hollywood Reporter reached out to Disney for comment, but did not hear back.

The Sprouse twins starred in Disney’s Suite Life shows as onscreen twins Cody and Zack Martin who lived in the Tipton Hotel. Song starred as London Tipton, daughter of hotel owner Wilfred Tipton; Lewis portrayed Marion Moseby, the manager of the hotel. Ashley Tisdale also starred in the show as Maddie Fitzpatrick, London’s friend and a Tipton Hotel employee.

Sprouse recently revealed on his podcast that he’s received only one residual check for five cents from his time working on The Suite Life of Zack & Cody.

“The only residual check I’ve ever received for The Suite Life at all was a crossover episode with Hannah Montana for five cents. I’m not joking,” he said. “Imagine that. In a time where residuals would have been great, not a single paycheck for being arguably one of the most popular shows.”

During that same episode of Sprouse’s podcast, he and Phill Lewis recalled shooting “wraparound episodes,” in which they’d film an additional scene from a future episode each week.

“A wraparound episode was we’d shoot our episode for the week and then they would say, ‘OK, we’re going to shoot this scene from an episode that you’re going to shoot in two weeks,’ and we’re like, ‘OK.’ So we shoot that scene. Then the next week they go, ‘You’re gonna shoot this scene from the same ep.’ And what they were doing was they were compiling an episode by shooting a scene a week, and then putting it together.”

When asked if it “counted” as a new episode, Sprouse said, “Not for us!” Lewis alleged, “We didn’t get paid for it. We weren’t paid for that episode.”

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Two goals from Domeiro Bobb-Semple, one in each half, put the Bees 2-0 up and a late debut goal for Connor Wickham wasn’t enough for the home side.

But just two days after their embarrassing FA Cup exit at Wimborne Town, it was another setback for interim boss Kevin Blackwell and his players.

Blackwell made 10 changes from that performance at the weekend against Wimborne. Only Vito Mannone kept his place, and new signing Ayman Benarous started the game, with the other new face Wickham starting on the bench.

In a fairly even first half, both sides played some good flowing football. However, it was the Bees who took the lead on 27 minutes. Omari Mrisho slipped in the penalty area, allowing Luca Picotto to cross, and then a bit of miscommunication between Ben Heneghan and Mannone allowed Bobb-Semple to tap into an empty net at the near post.

The Reds did have a couple of shots that were on target but neither of them really troubled Bees keeper Reggie Rose, as a couple of Pemi Aderoju headers were easily saved. Brentford also hit the outside of the post from a diving Isaac Holland header on 17 minutes.

Brentford started the second half the better, and it was only two minutes old when Mrisho fouled Luca Picotto in the box and referee Sunny Gill pointed to the spot. However, Isaac Holland hit the upright and the ball bounced away from danger.

It was soon 2-0 when yet another Mrisho error allowed Gustavo Gomez the space to pick out Bobb-Semple to fire home for his second goal of the night. The game settled down a little after that flurry of action.

Rose in the Brentford goal had to make a couple of saves, one from an Aderoju header, and he got a hand to a Klaudio Krasniqi shot, which was possibly going over anyway. Riley Owen fired a shot straight at Mannone midway through the second half.

Crawley’s other new signing, Wickham, managed to open his account for the Reds with a late penalty after Malcolm Ebowei was fouled in the box, to make it 2-1, but it wasn’t enough to put Crawley through.

Crawley now have 10 days off – their planned league game with Rotherham this weekend is postponed due to international call ups. The Reds next game is Fleetwood Town at home on November 21.

How to gain more Followers in Warhammer Survivors

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Gaining Followers in Warhammer Survivors has many benefits when it comes to the survival aspect of the game. Not only do they help you survive and last longer in the battle, but you’ll need to gain a minimum number if you want to complete the objectives.

While this method talks about gaining 5 followers, it can be any arbitrary number. I say 5 because that’s a requirement of an in-game objective that unlocks a crucial item. There is more than one way to gain followers, but this will be the easiest one for you.

First, unlock Trooper Bartosz Kozlowski and Sister Superior Amalia Novena, followed by their respective weapons. Now, begin a fresh run with Malum Caedo.

Once you’re deeper into your run, you’ll be offered the Lasgun and Shield of Faith as available weapons. Grab both of them, and then upgrade the Lasgun to Level 6. This will make Bartosz Kozlowski bring the required number of followers.

If you’re one short, that’s where Sister Amalia comes in. Once you acquire the Shield of Faith as a weapon, you’ll manage to have her as a companion. This will also unlock the Figure Case passive skill that allows you to return after dying.

You can repeat the same process with other characters in the game. Several characters allow you to add to the count of Followers when you unlock their signature weapon and level it up. However, the characters I have mentioned above are easy to unlock, and they will allow you to get to five Followers fast.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

When Sarah Paulson arrived at Vogue’s podcast studio to tape this Tuesday’s episode of The Run-Through, she did so in spectacular fashion. And we mean that literally: She was in the exact Miu Mu look that Olivia Colman had recently worn down the runway in Paris.

“It hasn’t been washed, so I’m getting all of her bodily oils,” Paulson jokes to Chloe Malle. “I’m hoping that there’s some process where if I just grip it tightly enough, it’s gonna get pressed into my being, and I will then acquire some of her gift.”

Of course, Paulson hardly needs the help; she is currently promoting Season 2 of All’s Fair, the legal drama created by Ryan Murphy in which she stars with Kim Kardashian, Niecy Nash, Teyana Taylor, Naomi Watts, and Glenn Close. Early next year, she will also return to Broadway in Bess Wohl’s Small Mouth Sounds.

With Season 13 of American Horror Story recently behind her, Paulson reveals that she’s said yes to every role Murphy has pitched her. (Consider that this year she also appeared in his Monster: The Lizzie Borden Story.) “When he offered me Marcia Clark”—Paulson’s ultimately Emmy-winning role in The People v. O.J. Simpson: American Crime Story—“I knew so many women were wanting it very aggressively,” she says. “And I don’t know why, to this day, he didn’t want a more famous person to do it. He was just really insistent that it be me. Doing that absolutely changed my life and changed my career totally.”

That win, coupled with her Tony for Branden Jacobs-Jenkins’s Appropriate, has put Paulson just two prizes away from the EGOT. “That’s not easy to do,” she notes good-humoredly. “Not everyone can be an ET!”

Forma Is a Wearable Viewfinder Built for Finding Photo Compositions

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Forma Is a Wearable Viewfinder Built for Finding Photo Compositions

Canadian photographer and inventor Peter Modzelewski has launched Forma on Kickstarter via his independent photo gear studio, Oramod. It is a pocket-sized optical viewfinder designed for a wide range of film and digital cameras.

Forma utilizes interchangeable, laser-cut cards tailored to specific formats and focal lengths, ranging from 35mm film cameras to large-format and specialized panoramic systems.

Photographers can simply hold Forma up to their eye to accurately preview a composition without setting up their camera—a process that can be quite an ordeal with certain medium- and large-format systems. It can also be used when scouting locations to help photographers determine where to position their gear in advance.

Forma can also be used on cameras without viewfinders, spanning specialized analog cameras to modern digital compacts like the Ricoh GR series.

As Modzelewski explains, Forma is constructed from a 3D-printed, carbon-fiber-infused composite material, offering a rugged yet lightweight design weighing just 30 grams (just over an ounce). It is built on the binocular fusion principle used in Voigtländer’s Kontur viewfinder from 1951. However, unlike the Kontur, Forma is highly configurable thanks to its interchangeable framing cards.

Forma features built-in illumination for low-light conditions, gels that color-tint the framelines for visibility in various environments, and a quick-release mounting system that allows photographers to switch between wearing it around the neck and attaching it to a camera in seconds.

“Worn around the neck, Forma helps photographers train their eye to recognize what their lenses will see, explore compositions before setting up a camera, and compare focal lengths by easily swapping cards,” Modzelewski says.

“Mounted on-camera, Forma provides a framing reference while allowing photographers to anticipate moments unfolding beyond the boundaries of a conventional viewfinder.”

Modzelewski sent pre-production units to photographers worldwide for real-world testing, including Japanese photographer Kazutoshi Yoshimura and British landscape photographer Lee Pengelly.

“I was amazed at how clearly I could see the field of view corresponding to the focal length of my lens,” Yoshimura says.

“It will be an essential part of my kit from now on,” adds Pengelly.

Utah-based large-format photographer James Holland touted Forma’s accuracy, noting that he didn’t have to move his tripod “an inch” when framing with it.

Forma is available to back on Kickstarter now, starting at $55 CAD (approximately $39 USD) for early birds. The campaign reached its funding goal quickly on launch day and has nearly 60 backers at the time of writing. Forma is expected to begin shipping to backers next month.

Trump Media Posts $410 Million Profit. Bitcoin Gains Did the Heavy Lifting

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Trump Media Posts $410 Million Profit. Bitcoin Gains Did the Heavy Lifting

Trump Media & Technology Group reported a $409.7 million third-quarter profit on Monday, a sharp turnaround for the company behind President Donald Trump's Truth Social.

However, most of the money that Trump Media & Technology made didn't come from media or technology. It came from the rising value of its crypto holdings and related investments.

The company generated just $2.97 million in revenue during the three months ended September 30, according to its SEC filing. A year earlier, it had reported a $54.8 million loss.

Trump Media recorded $496.1 million in realized and unrealized gains on digital assets, pledged crypto and equity securities, according to its earnings release. Much of that was an accounting gain—assets rose in value even when the company hadn't sold them for cash.

Digital assets and pledged coins accounted for $378.1 million of those gains, with securities adding another $118 million. Derivative losses and other expenses offset part of the windfall.

Bitcoin supplied a major boost. Chief Financial Officer Phillip Juhan said on the earnings call that it rose about 42% during the quarter, from roughly $59,000 at the end of June to $84,000 at the end of September.

Trump Media held approximately 15,649 Bitcoin at quarter-end, including around 11,385 coins pledged to a program designed to generate additional returns. Some of those assets are subject to arrangements allowing the counterparty to reuse the coins, according to the filing.

The company expanded into crypto in July 2025, purchasing about $2 billion in Bitcoin and related securities. That decision has tied its earnings closely to changes in digital-asset prices.

The downside was obvious just three months ago. Back in August Trump Media lost $238 million in the second quarter as declining crypto prices battered its holdings. This quarter, the accounting effect went in the opposite direction.

Truth Social's underlying business improved, though from a small base. Revenue increased 205% from about $973,000 a year earlier, supported by subscriptions for Truth+ streaming and Truth API, a paid service that gives customers faster access to posts on the platform that critics have likened to insider trading.

The company also recorded growth in advertising sales and fees from its Truth.Fi funds. Even so, Trump Media reported a $12.3 million adjusted EBITDA loss, a measure that excludes investment gains and certain other items, compared with a $29.1 million loss a year earlier.

The latest quarterly profit has not repaired the longer-term picture for shareholders. DJT shares traded around $4.20 Tuesday, up about 3.3% on the day but down 94.7% from their $79.38 registered back in 2024. The stock has lost nearly 70% this year.

Trump Media still posted a $234.2 million net loss for the first nine months of 2026. It is also pursuing a $6 billion merger with fusion-energy developer TAE Technologies, which shareholders are scheduled to vote on December 14.

Before that vote, holders of the company's $1 billion in convertible notes can demand cash repayment on November 30, according to its quarterly filing.

water becomes a way of understanding the world in this powerhouse parramatta installation

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

developed around stories and cultural practices shared by seven aboriginal organizations across new south wales, 'overflow' turns water, light and ancient sandstone into an environment that visitors experience barefoot.

Tyrrell Studio transforms a naturally lit gallery at the recently opened Powerhouse Parramatta, Australia, into a shifting landscape of water, sandstone and reflected light with Overflow, a large-scale installation grounded in the living principles of First Nations Water Country. Conceived by Wiradjuri woman Emily McDaniel, the First Nations-led exhibition brings together stories from seven Aboriginal organizations across New South Wales, with films by Kamilaroi filmmaker Ivan Sen. The project makes water the physical medium through which visitors encounter these stories, inviting them to remove their shoes and walk barefoot across a changing tidal landscape.

Occupying a gallery measuring approximately 60 × 40 meters, the installation takes the form of a shallow pool flowing across an abstracted sandstone rock shelf. The stone references the geological terrain beneath Parramatta, where the river has shaped the landscape over tens of thousands of years. As water rises and recedes, different sections of the sculpted surface emerge and disappear, transforming the gallery throughout the day.

Reflections of the building’s structure and surrounding sky appear on the surface of the pool, while natural daylight creates shifting patterns across the space. As the water level rises, the installation becomes a broad reflective plane; as it retreats, the sandstone outcrops become visible again.

The artwork by the team at Tyrrell Studio draws on a broader understanding of the Parramatta River as a changing landscape. Before European settlement, the river occupied an extensive terrain shaped by tidal movement, sediment and the meeting of freshwater and saltwater. Its banks were not the definitive boundaries that contemporary infrastructure suggests but part of a much larger system extending through sand, stone, groundwater and floodplains.

European settlement gradually changed this relationship. Weirs, built-up riverbanks and concrete channels were introduced to control the flow of water, turning a naturally changing river into a more contained and controlled waterway. Tyrrell Studio’s design responds to this history, reconsidering what lies beyond the visible channel.

Overflow allows some of the underlying processes of the former landscape to become perceptible. Water gathers, spreads and withdraws across the stone and changing light and reflections introduce an element of unpredictability. The installation brings attention to the forces that continue to shape the terrain beneath the contemporary city.

The cultural foundations of the exhibition come from Emily McDaniel’s original concept, which places Aboriginal cultural practices at the center of the experience. Seven participating organizations contribute knowledge and stories connected to Water Country across New South Wales, including Baramadagal Community, Gamay Rangers, The Goondee Aboriginal Keeping Place and Museum, Jagun Alliance, Joonga Land and Water Aboriginal Corporation, Moogahlin Performing Arts and Naru Aboriginal Corporation.

Their work encompasses caring for species, fishing, water monitoring, flood mitigation, language and cultural water rights, practices that enter the gallery through films by Ivan Sen. Sen’s films bring different waterways, communities, and forms of knowledge into conversation with the physical environment in Parramatta, projected onto and reflected across the water, where ripples fragment their images before they reform across the sandstone and the bodies of visitors.

Overflow is closely connected to Tyrrell Studio’s work outside the museum, particularly its permanent 3,000-square-meter riverfront landscape, Ngurra Budyari Ngalawagulang. Developed with Baramadagal elders and Dharug community representatives, the public space draws on the cultural and natural history of Water Country. The landscape is made up of sandstone paths, vegetation and rain gardens. Water collects in shallow hollows, plants grow through the paving and the stone gradually changes with exposure to the elements. Τhe design lets nature become part of how the space looks and feels. It is a landscape that changes with the weather, the seasons and the passage of time.

Inside the museum, Overflow picks up where the riverfront leaves off. The same materials and ideas continue into the gallery, where water moves across sculpted sandstone instead of being contained within a pool. The connection can even be felt in the morning, when mist from the Parramatta River rises toward the upper floors of the museum.

name: Overflow location: Powerhouse Parramatta | @powerhousemuseum, Sydney, Australia exhibition design: Tyrrell Studio | @tyrrellstudio concept creator: Emily McDaniel film: Ivan Sen client: Powerhouse photography: Mark Tyrrell Tyrrell Studio design team: Mark Tyrrell, Sarah Yates, Gabrielle Veringa, Hadi Abu Ammar, Dan Sharp commissioned communities and organizations: Baramadagal Community, Gamay Rangers, The Goondee Aboriginal Keeping Place and Museum, Jagun Alliance, Joonga Land and Water Aboriginal Corporation, Moogahlin Performing Arts, Naru Aboriginal Corporation Powerhouse team: Lisa Havilah, Beau James, Clare Holland, Marco Rinaldi, Ivan Muniz Reed

Apple to investigate accident that killed three workers at iPhone parts factory in India

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Reuters reports that three contract workers died after inhaling gas during an inspection at a Tata Electronics factory that produces iPhone components in India. Here are the details.

As frequent 9to5Mac readers probably know, Apple’s supply chain in India relies heavily on Tata, which manufactures iPhone components and accounts for a significant share of the country’s iPhone assembly operations.

Since Apple began diversifying its manufacturing operations away from China, India has emerged as a major production hub, with roughly a quarter of the world’s iPhones now manufactured in the country.

Earlier this year, Apple found itself linked to an environmental controversy in India, after the Tamil Nadu Pollution Control Board investigated allegations that wastewater from a Tata facility had contaminated nearby farmland water.

A few weeks later, however, Tata said the regulator had dropped further action against the facility after the company addressed the concerns raised.

At the time, Tata said it had ordered separate testing related to the facility and that the results supported its position that the plant was operating within the rules.

However, the matter was apparently not fully resolved, as just three days later, Reuters reported that local health officials were still investigating complaints from nearby farmers.

And while there had been no major developments reported about the wastewater investigation since then, Reuters reported today that three contract workers died in an accident at the same Tata facility.

The three contract workers died during maintenance work on an effluent treatment ​plant operated by Nalco Water at the Tata facility, which is located in Hosur in Tamil Nadu, ​district official Dinesh Kumar said, adding that the deaths occurred when the workers inhaled gas while inspecting an external nano-filtration tank.

Reuters added that “Apple’s team is visiting ​the plant to assist in ​the ongoing investigation,” though the company did not respond when reached for comment.

The report added that Tata and Tamil Nadu authorities are also investigating the fatal accident, and noted that “production was not affected as the facility was closed for the Hindu festival of Deepavali this week.”

Can crypto trust firms compete with banks in US derivatives settlements?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Crypto custodians are moving into a market handling billions in daily settlements, but regulatory relief and a shift in congressional control leave questions over their long-term role.

Crypto companies are moving deeper into financial services traditionally dominated by banks, seeking a role in US derivatives settlements, where billions of dollars change hands daily.

The push comes as regulators expand the services trust companies can provide, even as traditional banking groups challenge parts of the regulatory framework governing crypto firms’ expansion into banklike activities.

A Nov. 5 decision by the Commodity Futures Trading Commission (CFTC) illustrates the shift, allowing derivatives clearinghouses to use trust companies for daily settlements with clearing members, a function previously restricted to banks under the agency’s regulations.

The relief could create new business opportunities for regulated crypto custodians, but its revocable nature and changes in the US political landscape raise questions about the durability of the regulatory opening.

The scale of the derivatives settlement business offers some perspective on the opportunity.

CME Group, one of the world’s largest derivatives market operators, transferred an average of $8.7 billion per business day through its clearing systems during the first half of 2026, up from $6.7 billion a day in 2025, according to regulatory filings.

Those figures represent funds transferred between clearing firms to settle gains and losses, rather than fees earned by settlement banks. They nevertheless illustrate the scale of financial flows supported by the infrastructure that trust companies could increasingly help provide.

The CFTC’s Division of Clearing and Risk issued no-action relief on Nov. 5 following an Oct. 29 request from derivatives clearinghouse Nodal Clear, allowing clearinghouses to use trust companies for daily settlements if they meet the same evaluation and monitoring requirements applied to traditional settlement banks.

The decision addresses an inconsistency between federal commodities law, which permits customer funds to be held at banks or trust companies, and CFTC Regulation 39.14, which defines settlement banks only as banks.

Nodal Clear partnered with Coinbase in 2025 to integrate USDC into US futures trading. Coinbase Custody Trust was already expected to provide daily settlement services under the arrangement, Cointelegraph has learned.

Related: CFTC expands regulatory relief for passive trading software providers

Other crypto firms have also expanded their institutional financial services. On Nov. 4, BitGo launched its Global Markets division, combining derivatives trading, financing, collateral management and settlement capabilities.

Coinbase’s national trust bank charter also took effect on Nov. 6, allowing Coinbase National Trust Company, a separate entity from Coinbase Custody Trust, to provide federally supervised digital asset custody services.

Several major crypto firms have also secured US national trust bank approvals over the past months, including Circle, Ripple, Crypto.com and Stripe-owned Bridge. The charters, granted by the Office of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC), reflect the industry’s expansion into federally regulated financial services.

The expansion of specialized trust companies has prompted objections from traditional banking groups.

In October, the Independent Community Bankers of America sued the OCC, arguing that its national trust bank charter rules could allow crypto firms to conduct certain banklike activities without meeting all the requirements imposed on conventional banks.

The lawsuit reflects a broader disagreement over how specialized financial institutions should be regulated as they expand into services also provided by banks.

Cody Carbone, CEO of The Digital Chamber, told Cointelegraph that the CFTC’s relief does not put trust companies in a better or worse position than traditional banks.

Trust companies and banks already compete for services permitted under their respective charters, Carbone said, adding that the decision gives clearinghouses more options without preventing banks from retaining their business. “A trust company charter is not a regulatory shortcut,” he said.

Despite the potential opportunities, the CFTC’s decision leaves questions about how securely trust companies can establish a long-term role in derivatives settlement.

The agency issued a no-action letter rather than formally amending its regulations. The letter states that CFTC staff will not recommend enforcement action against clearinghouses using trust companies for daily settlements if specified conditions are met.

Unlike a formal rule amendment, which generally requires a public notice-and-comment process, staff no-action relief does not change the underlying regulation and can be withdrawn.

“A change in Commission leadership could withdraw the CFTC Letter. More formal Commission action, such as amending the regulation, would be necessary to avoid this potential risk,” Jordan Yeagley, a shareholder at Buchanan Ingersoll & Rooney, told Cointelegraph.

The uncertainty comes at a politically significant moment for the agency. CFTC Chair Michael Selig remains the regulator’s sole commissioner, despite a statutory structure providing for five commissioners. Selig has led the agency since December 2025, leaving four seats vacant as the regulator expands its work on digital asset markets.

The political environment is also changing following the Nov. 3 US midterm elections, in which Democrats won control of both chambers of Congress.

The incoming Congress, which takes office in January, will have greater power to scrutinize the CFTC’s regulatory agenda. The Senate also has a role in confirming nominees to fill the agency’s vacant commissioner seats, while the House and Senate Agriculture Committees oversee the regulator.

Sen. Sherrod Brown, who won Ohio’s Senate race on Nov. 3 despite more than $60 million in opposition spending from crypto-backed political action committees, could take on a prominent role on the Senate Agriculture Committee.

The Best Gooloo Portable Jump Starters Do More Than Just Bring an Engine Back to Life

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The compact A3, value-packed A5, and heavy-duty A7 all punch above their weight in power capacity and air pressure, plus offer essentials like a flashlight and charging ports.

Gear-obsessed editors choose every product we review. We may earn commission if you buy from a link. Why Trust Us?

If you’re looking for a reliable portable jump starter, Gooloo is one of the first names that comes to mind. The brand has a reputation for offering models with impressive jump-starting power and capacity, which I’ve confirmed in my testing.

While Gooloo has plenty of portable jump starters in its lineup, three in particular are worth comparing for features relative to price point: the compact 3,000-amp A3, the value-packed 4,000-amp A5, and the heavy-duty 6,000-amp A7.

Along with at least double the amps required to jump most vehicles, large trucks, and SUVs, Gooloo’s devices stand out for more than the ability to bring an engine back to life. Many, including the models I mention above, double as tire inflators and include advanced functions, such as automatic modes for, say, automobile and bicycle tires, and auto shutoff when a user-set pressure is reached. They’re also packed with a variety of handy features, like flashlights and USB ports.

The 3,000-amp Gooloo A3 is the smallest and lightest of the three portable jump starters, but it’s mighty. The tire inflator has 1.24 cubic feet per minute (cfm) and 150 psi ratings that are impressive for the A3’s size, which means it can fill a 17-inch tire (common in most passenger vehicles) from 30 to 36 psi in under one minute. A 2.8-inch color screen simplifies stepping through five preset inflation modes: SUV, car, bike, ball, and custom.

Like the Gooloo units below, the jump starter has a boost button and 10 protection features, including for short-circuit (reversing the leads when connected to a battery) and overheating. There are various nozzles for inflating tires, sports equipment such as balls, and other items.

Features include a 30-watt USB-C input/output port, a USB-A output, an LED flashlight, and a USB-A to USB-C cable to charge its internal battery. For proper storage, there is a sturdy soft-sided carrying case. One downside: I found the air compressor louder than the other Gooloo units here and some of the other tire inflators I’ve tested.

The Gooloo A5 is a little larger and heavier; however, it ups the ante with 4,000 amps of peak power and 20,000 milliampere-hours (mAh) of capacity that can crank even the largest passenger vehicles and retain a charge for more than a year. The tire inflator is rated 160 psi, and Gooloo doesn’t provide a cfm spec but says the A5 can inflate a 15-inch car tire from 30 to 36 psi in 30 seconds. In my testing, it was noticeably faster and quieter than the A3. Like the A3, though, it has five automatic inflation modes, automatically stops when a preset inflation pressure is reached, and comes with five nozzles.

The A5’s 3.2-inch color display is my favorite of the three units. It’s easy to read, even in bright sunlight, and a big plus is its 45-watt USB-C charging cable. It’s attached at one corner so that you won’t lose it, plus it doubles as a carrying handle.

There’s also a USB-A output port for charging devices such as a phone or tablet, a three-mode flashlight, and a USB-A to USB-C charging cable that’s used to recharge the unit from 0 to 100 percent in about six hours. It also comes with a decent storage case.

The Gooloo A7 is larger and heavier than the A3 and A5, and I found it slightly more stable than the A3 and A5 thanks to its square footprint.

At 6,000 amps, it packs the most power and has 24,000 mAh of capacity so that it can crank larger engines and hold a charge longer. But at almost three times the price without an appreciable increase in power, the only way to justify its much higher cost is with the significantly robust tire inflator with a 5.30 cfm rating that’s more than double that of any of the portable tire inflators I’ve previously tested.

It’s also the only unit here that has a very powerful 12.4-cfm “blower” function that, along with a supplied hose and four adapters, can be used to quickly inflate and deflate large items such as a blow-up mattress or stand-up paddleboard.

As with the A3 and A5, it also has five inflation presets as well as automatic shutoff when the set inflation pressure is reached, a three-mode flashlight, and a boost button. It has a USB-C input/output for charging the device and powering other portable electronics, as well as a USB-A output port.

The 3.7-inch display is the largest of the three units, making it quickly readable at a glance, although the display isn’t as vibrant as the A5’s color display. It’s a disappointment that the device comes with a cheap nylon carrying bag instead of the sturdier cases provided with the A3 and A5. An appreciated perk, though, is a supplied set of lightweight work gloves to protect your hands and keep them from getting dirty while using the device to jump-start an engine or inflate a tire.

All three of these combination portable jump starters will get you out of a jam, and each has advantages and disadvantages. The A3 is a great choice for the majority of motorists. A friend of mine chose this device for his motorcycle because of its compact size. I recommend the A5 for its impressive capabilities compared to its price—and it has the best display. The higher cost of the A7 is only worth it if you own a larger vehicle or need the inflate-and-deflate blower function. But no matter which one you pick, you’ll be relieved you have it on hand the next time you’re stranded with a dead battery or deflated tire, or worse yet, both.

Other Portable Jump Starter Combos We Recommend

The Noco delivers an impressive 2,000 amps of peak power and has 16,000 mAh capacity, while the tire inflator pumps out 2.3 cfm and up to 100 psi. It has auto shutoff and memory settings like other air compressors I’ve tested but adds “dwell” technology that pauses and then resumes inflation when a set pressure is reached to ensure proper inflation. Its 60-watt USB-C port recharges the device from 0 to 100 percent in about an hour.

With 4,000 amps of peak current and 24,000 mAh of capacity, the Wolfbox can jump-start up to 10.0-liter gas or 6.0-liter diesel engines. It holds a charge for more than a year. The 1.5-cfm air pump takes one minute to inflate a 17-inch tire from 30 to 36 psi. It also has a 65-watt USB-C in/out port to quickly recharge the internal battery, two USB-A ports to charge other devices and includes a 400-lumen, three-mode LED flashlight.

With 1,200 amps, this device cranks up to 6.0-liter gas and 3.0-liter diesel engines. Spark and reverse-polarity protection won’t send current to the battery clamps unless they’re securely and correctly connected. A built-in pump provides 140 psi and comes with adapters for bikes, balls, and other items. Included are a USB-C charging cable and port, a USB-A outlet, a clamshell carrying case, and lifetime customer service.

Doug Newcomb splits his time between Hood River, Ore., and Todos Santos, Mexico. He's been writing about technology and cars for more than 30 years for Wired, Rolling Stone, Road & Track, Automobile, PCMag.com, Playboy and numerous other publications. He cofounded the C3 Group in 2013 to produce automotive industry events in New York, San Francisco, Washington, D.C. and at SXSW in Austin. C3 Group was acquired by Informa in 2018. When he's not writing, he's riding a surfboard

Broadway Box Office: ‘Much Ado About Nothing’ Hits $2.3 Million As Industry Rebounds

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

'Here There Are Blueberries' and 'It's A Wonderful Life' began previews, as industry grosses rose 24 percent.

Much Ado About Nothing, starring Tom Hiddleston and Hayley Atwell, shot up to $2.3 million in its first week of eight performances, making it the third highest grossing show of the week, and suggesting it will be a top earner going forward. Capacity was at 100 percent for the show, which is set to open Nov. 19.

The average ticket price for Much Ado About Nothing fell back down to $194, still putting it in the upper echelon, but several rungs below the $315 average ticket price at Other Desert Cities.

Hamilton was the highest grossing show, with $2.7 million, followed by the starry Other Desert Cities, with Julia Louis-Dreyfus, Allison Janney and Joe Keery, with $2.5 million, Much Ado About Nothing, The Lion King with $1.8 million and Harry Potter and the Cursed Child with $1.7 million.

Billy Crystal’s one-man show, 860, remains a top-earner and is rising in gross, with $1.5 million brought in last week. A Few Good Men also remains in the top tier, with $1.44 million last week, despite mixed reviews, as does the Bowen Yang-led Oh, Mary! with $1.4 million. The Lost Boys, which opened last season, also continues to draw in crowds, edging out past favorites including Wicked, which has seen its grosses gradually drop throughout 2026.

New musical Wanted opened on Nov. 8 to mixed reviews. In the heavily comped week in the leadup, as well as the fully comped opening night, the show brought in $517,134 and played to 94 percent capacity.

Several shows were in previews last week, including Downstate, and The Fanstasticks. Here There Are Blueberries began previews last week, with 99 percent capacity and $669,084 across seven performances. Holiday radio play It’s a Wonderful Life, starring Andrew Rannells, Megan Hilty, Tituss Burgess, Lisa Rinna and Harry Hamlin, played its first six performances last week. The show had a somewhat shaky start with 78 percent capacity and a gross of $322,788.10.

Overall the industry gross rose 24 percent and attendance 15 percent thanks to the addition of the two new shows, as well as the rebound from Halloween and marathon weekend.

Milan’s Cisse out to emulate Italy icon Balotelli

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Alphadjo Cisse is hoping to emulate his Italy icon Mario Balotelli, with the AC Milan youngster set to potentially make his senior international debut at San Siro this week.

Cisse was handed his first Azzurri call-up ahead of their Nations League fixtures versus France and Belgium, following a strong start to his time with the Rossoneri.

He has scored eight goals in 13 appearances for Milan, who he joined in February before immediately heading back on loan to Serie B side Catanzaro for the remainder of 2025-26.

Only nine of those appearances have been starts, and he has played just 659 minutes in total, while no other Milan player has scored more than five goals in all competitions this term (Adrien Rabiot).

The 20-year-old spoke to reporters on Tuesday, two days out from a potential Azzurri debut, and he was asked who his Italy idol was.

He responded: "Without a doubt, I'd say Balotelli. He's always been a role model for me on the national team."

Balotelli scored 14 goals in 36 senior caps for his country, with the highlight of his Italy career being a memorable goal against Germany in the Euro 2012 semi-finals.

Liberali and Cisse speak following their call-ups  #Azzurri #VivoAzzurro https://t.co/yIbERrwv9Z

If Roberto Mancini opts to use Cisse against France, his senior international bow would come at the home of his club, a moment he would relish.

"The coach will make the decision. Returning to San Siro with the national team would be even more special," Cisse said.

"I'm very ambitious, and playing for the national team is a source of pride. As I said before, I think I have broad shoulders.

"Many players in the national team have been here a long time and have also won the European Championship.

"I respect them, and we have to take things step by step. My first thought is to make my debut, and then we'll think about everything else later."

Italy have seven points on the board in Nations League Group A1 and would leapfrog France to go top with a win over Les Bleus, but even then, they may need a result against Belgium to ensure qualification for the quarter-finals.

Saliba may struggle to dislodge Konsa on Arsenal return, says Ferdinand

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

William Saliba may struggle to get back into Arsenal's starting XI when he returns to full fitness, given the hugely impressive form of recent signing Ezri Konsa.

That is the view of former Manchester United and England centre-back Rio Ferdinand, who believes Konsa's transition since arriving from Aston Villa has been seamless.

Saliba has been sidelined since suffering a back injury during France's defeat to Spain in the World Cup semi-finals, though he played for Arsenal's under-21s last weekend as he nears a return.

But with Konsa joining the Premier League champions and slotting in alongside Gabriel Magalhaes, Mikel Arteta's side have barely noticed Saliba's absence.

Arsenal boast a five-point lead over Brighton at the top of the table, having won nine of their first 10 Premier League matches, and their defensive record has been nothing short of fearsome.

The Gunners have conceded the fewest goals (five), given up the lowest expected goals against figure (7.6 xGA), faced the fewest shots on target (26) and allowed their opponents the lowest xG figure per shot (0.7) and conversation rate (4.5%) among all Premier League sides.

Konsa has appeared in nine of their games, making the joint-most interceptions among their squad (nine, alongside Gabriel and Declan Rice) and winning possession 32 times – only Rice (35 times) has recovered the ball more often.

Konsa will miss England's meeting with Croatia on Thursday but could return from a minor knock in time to face Spain on Sunday, and if the 29-year-old stays fit, Ferdinand feels Arteta will find it difficult to drop him.

Ferdinand said on his YouTube channel: "Mikel Arteta has got a huge problem now. What is he going to do when Saliba is fit?

"Because Konsa has come into that back four and said, 'I have been here for 10 years and I am the immovable object now,' that's what it feels like.

Another top display. Another clean sheet. In Focus: Ezri Konsa edition

"He has just slid in there and said, 'that is my spot, it is mine now'. He has made some last-ditch tackles to keep them in games and to help them win games.

"He looks dominant, aggressive, and he looks accustomed to wearing the Arsenal shirt. He looks like he is meant to be there, but let's not forget about Saliba and what he was doing when he was in the team, how great he looked.

"This is a problem, but it is a problem that Mikel Arteta, I hope, will enjoy – to have two top centre-backs vying to play in that team, who are performing at their best at the moment, and arguably your best one [Saliba] is not in there… what a position to be in."

Arsenal visit Newcastle United for their first game back after the international break, on November 21, before hosting Manchester City in a huge clash between title rivals the following week.
