853 Runners Were Disqualified From This Road Race. Many Broke the Same Starting-Line Rule.

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

More than 3 percent of finishers lost official results after organizers reviewed bib data.

Your race bib can tell organizers a lot more than how quickly you finished. At Spain's 61st edition of Behobia–San Sebastián, it also helped them figure out whether you started when you were supposed to. As it turns out, getting a little too eager at the starting line can be enough to cost you an official result.

Organizers confirmed that 853 participants were disqualified from the November 8 event, a popular 20K race that attracted 27,172 runners this year. According to Spanish outlets Mundo Deportivo and 3Cat, starting in an earlier wave than assigned was a major reason behind the unusually high number.

The race sorts participants into groups based on their previous performances, with staggered start times designed to help manage the crowds. Runners can move back to a later wave if they wish, but jumping ahead to an earlier one is grounds for disqualification. It's a rule that should sound familiar to anyone who's run a race with NYRR, as they follow the same policy and warn that moving into a faster wave or corral can cost runners their results.

With thousands of people lining up, slipping into an earlier group can go unnoticed, but timing chips attached to race bibs allow organizers to track when participants cross the starting line and other checkpoints along the course.

Other violations, including using another runner's bib without an authorized transfer, are against the rules and can also result in disqualification. However, organizers haven't provided explanations for every case, making it unclear how many involved starting-wave violations.

According to figures released by organizers, 29,301 people registered for the event, and 26,947 crossed the 12.4-mile course’s finish line in San Sebastián. Of those who completed the race, a little more than 3 percent were disqualified.

While it sounds like a lot, 853 is actually an improvement compared to previous years. In 2023, a whopping 1,327 runners were disqualified, followed by 958 in 2024 and 918 last year. So the numbers are heading in the right direction, although there's clearly still some confusion—or disregard—when it comes to following the pre-start rules.

Runner’s World has contacted race organizers for details about the disqualifications, including whether affected participants have an opportunity to appeal.

Sean Abrams was the Senior Editor, Growth and Engagement at Men’s Health. He’s a former hip hop dancer who likes long walks on the beach and large glasses of tequila. You can find his previous work at Maxim, Elite Daily, and AskMen.

Lufthansa Takes Delivery of Its First A350-1000

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Lufthansa this week took delivery of its first Airbus A350-1000, a type it says will offer a better customer experience while reducing operating costs.

The aircraft was formally handed over to the carrier on Tuesday in the presence of Lufthansa Group CEO Carsten Spohr and Lars Wagner, CEO of commercial aircraft at Airbus. Nicknamed “Deutschland,” the jet is Lufthansa’s 700th Airbus airplane, and the seventh in a special commemorative fleet bearing 100th anniversary liveries.

The white and dark blue designs feature an oversized crane, a symbol first used by predecessor Deutsche Luft Hansa.

Lufthansa Group has ordered a total of 15 A350-1000s. The first 10 will operate for the Lufthansa core brand, and will be based at its Munich hub.

“Deutschland” will fly to Munich on Wednesday, officials said, and operate its inaugural flight, to Tokyo-Haneda, on Dec. 3. The aircraft will also be Lufthansa’s first long-haul airplane equipped with Starlink satellite internet service.

“The fact that, as the world’s largest Airbus customer, we are taking delivery of our 700th Airbus aircraft exactly 50 years after the first Airbus delivery to Lufthansa, and in the year of our 100th anniversary, makes this a very special moment for us,” Spohr said in a statement. “Our partnership is a prime example of what Europe can achieve when it works together.”

Lufthansa’s -1000s will seat 300 passengers in four cabins – First, Business, Premium Economy, and Economy.

This Is the Jarred Pasta Sauce Ina Garten Always Keeps in Her Pantry

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

"Store-bought is fine" carries a little more weight when Ina tells you exactly which pasta sauce to buy.

I can happily spend an entire afternoon making dinner. Give me a little music, a glass of wine and something to braise, and I’ll settle in without much concern for when it all reaches the table. It’s a lovely way to cook when I have the time, but leisurely cooking isn’t always practical for busy parents. On most days, dinner needs to fit into my family’s schedule, and my kids don’t want to hear that dinner is still achieving important levels of depth and complexity as it enters its sixth hour on the stove.

But having less time to cook shouldn’t lower our expectations for dinner. A simple plate of pasta can be every bit as satisfying—provided the ingredients involved are worth eating. It’s why I’m particular about store-bought sauce. I’m happy to buy it, but I want to enjoy it as much as anything I spent time making myself.

Ina Garten seems to have arrived at a similar conclusion. Her signature “store-bought is fine” reassurance comes with some excellent recommendations, including the marinara from Rao’s Homemade, a brand I’ve been loyal to for years. It’s a small thing to have in common with Ina, but until I acquire a Parisian apartment of my own, I’ll gladly take it.

“Why make your own marinara when Rao’s marinara sauce tastes homemade?” Ina has said. It’s a reasonable question, particularly coming from someone with very little left to prove in the kitchen.

She’s equally decisive on her website. When a reader asked her favorite jarred marinara, Ina answered, “That’s easy! Rao’s Marinara Sauce.” It also appears among her recommended ingredients in the site’s shopping section.

Her advice to an incoming college student gives the recommendation some practical context. When asked which staples to have on hand, Ina suggested keeping pasta and Rao’s marinara around for a ten-minute dinner. That’s useful advice at any age, particularly once you discover that graduation doesn’t necessarily leave you with more time to cook.

Ina’s comment about homemade flavor gets at why I’ve continued to buy it, too: Rao’s has the richness and depth I want in a sauce, even when I’m serving it with nothing more elaborate than bucatini and Parmesan. I actually look forward to that dinner. The fact that it comes together quickly is just a bonus.

Lately, I’ve been especially impressed with the brand’s new creamy roasted garlic sauce (so much so that I gave it a Taste of Home Award). The mellow roasted garlic and the richness of mascarpone have made it a favorite in my house.

Ina’s explanation of “good ingredients” offers a useful starting point. In Modern Comfort Food, she writes, “They don’t have to be expensive, but they need to be chosen thoughtfully.” Her emphasis is on flavor and choosing ingredients because of what they contribute to the finished dish.

When it comes to Rao’s, she points to slow simmering and good tomatoes, basil, garlic and oregano. That makes the ingredient label a sensible place to begin when comparing jars. The marinara label lists whole peeled Italian tomatoes, followed by olive oil, onions, salt, garlic, basil, black pepper and oregano—and no added sugar.

For a classic marinara, look for that familiar foundation of tomatoes, oil, aromatics and herbs. If you prefer a more savory sauce, keep your eye on the added sugar, too. These are useful details to compare across brands, although the label can only tell you so much about how well the ingredients have been cooked and balanced. In this case, I’m happy to let Ina’s recommendation settle the matter.

Ava Labs exec says NYSE deal marks end of blockchain’s ‘general purpose era’

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Ava Labs’ John Nahas said the collaboration with the NYSE shows institutions moving past pilots toward purpose-built onchain market infrastructure.

Traditional finance is moving past one-size-fits-all blockchains, according to John Nahas, chief business officer at Ava Labs, the company behind the Avalanche network.

Nahas told Cointelegraph that the planned NYSE-affiliated tokenized securities platform, which will use Avalanche technology for clearing and settlement, illustrates that financial institutions want more control over how their blockchains operate.

“Capital markets need reliable performance and predictable cost settlement, certainty and control over the network and its participants,” he said. “It’s really important for this chain to be purpose built for the needs of NYSE.”

Nahas argued that the crypto industry’s preference for general-purpose blockchains and its reluctance to adapt to the compliance rules financial institutions face have slowed adoption.

“Crypto always wants to put the crypto part front and center,” Nahas said. He sees a shift toward blockchains designed for specific use cases. “I look at this as the end of the general purpose era,” he said.

Related: Avalanche’s AVAX leads crypto gains following NYSE tokenization deal

The NYSE move, announced on Monday, comes as more companies object to third parties tokenizing their shares for trading on OKXICE, a separate venue proposed by crypto exchange OKX and NYSE parent Intercontinental Exchange (ICE). OKXICE listed 67 companies that had objected as of Nov. 5, including Apple, Amazon, Meta and Tesla.

Nahas put the pushback down partly to a lack of understanding among incumbents, who he said see crypto as a speculative “wild west.” He said a “wholesale change” to market infrastructure built around institutions’ needs would make the potential efficiency gains clearer and expected objecting companies would “come around.”

Dennis Lee, the NYSE’s head of digital issuance, said the exchange will be “picking the right technology for each job,” as its develops the platform.

Although the NYSE-affiliated platform will support equities issued on a variety of blockchains, the settlement ledger had to allow the exchange to “control who can take part and keep costs predictable,” both elements Lee said Avalanche met. On public blockchains, such as Ethereum, institutions can restrict access to their own applications, while the underlying networks remain open. Congestion can also drive up transaction fees.

The NYSE spent a year testing Avalanche, giving Ava Labs time to tailor its approach to the exchange’s needs, Nahas said.

“The problem is general purpose chains don’t want to or most of the time cannot adapt to the existing frameworks,” he said.

Nahas said the ability to adapt matters most when regulations are still taking shape. “Nobody wants to be on the wrong side of a line that didn’t exist the day before,” he said. In his view, building around the rules financial institutions already follow can help projects move forward while regulators clarify how those rules apply to crypto.

Asked what the “blue wave” in the House and Senate could mean for the US crypto industry, Nahas said he remained optimistic. “The landscape has changed, but the underlying fact is that this train has left the station,” he said. “You see that today in a big way.”

Fairgames is PlayStation’s latest cancellation as it steps back from many of its live service efforts.﻿

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Posts from this author will be added to your daily email digest and your homepage feed.

Sony has canceled Fairgames, a heist game announced in 2023, according to Bloomberg. Fairgames had once been part of Sony’s big planned push for PlayStation live service games, but the company ended up canceling some planned live service titles, including in the God of War and The Last of Us franchises, and it shut down Concord shortly after its failed launch.

The Fairgames cancellation won’t result in job cuts, and developer Haven Studios will now support other PlayStation studios, Bloomberg reports. The game was canceled following a recent test where “player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set,” according to part of an executive memo published by Bloomberg, which also cited “challenging market conditions in the PVP shooter genre.” Sony acquired Haven Studios in 2022.

Sony’s live service efforts have seen some success with Helldivers 2, which is also available on Xbox. Bungie’s Marathon, which launched this year, has had a rough start, but today the studio showed off some new features coming in a December update, including a new co-op PvE roguelite mode and a PvP deathmatch mode. Sony is also working on a live service Horizon spinoff, but recently rebooted it.

Barcelona Women vs Arsenal Women - prediction, team news, lineups

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

By Ben Sully | 10 Nov 2026 21:06, Last updated: 10 Nov 2026 21:08

Arsenal Women will travel to the Camp Nou for Thursday's highly anticipated meeting with reigning European Champions Barcelona Women.

The Gunners are sitting in fifth spot in the Women's Champions League league phase, while the Catalan giants are flying high at the top of the standings with a perfect record.

Barcelona have made the perfect start to their bid to win a fifth UWCL title, having won their opening three league phase games by a combined 19-2 scoreline.

After comfortably dispatching Paris FC and Roma, Barcelona made light work of their clash against Koge on matchday three, with Claudia Pina netting a brace in an emphatic 8-0 victory in Denmark.

Barcelona returned to Liga F action with a 4-0 success against fellow Catalan side Badalona, before they eased to a 5-1 victory in Sunday's meeting with Madrid CFF, moving them four points clear of second-placed Real Madrid at the top of the standings.

Those two league wins have helped Barcelona build a four-game winning run since suffering a surprise 2-1 away loss against Real Sociedad on October 18.

While they experienced a blip on their travels, Barcelona have won all six of their competitive home games this season, scoring at least five goals in five of those victories.

The Blaugrana have also won 33 of their 35 UWCL home games (D1, L1), including each of their last 11 European home assignments since losing 1-0 to Chelsea in the first leg of their semi-final tie in April 2024.

Arsenal, meanwhile, have struggled for consistency in domestic action this season, with Renee Slegers's side sitting in sixth spot and six points adrift of the top three after recording three wins, four draws and two defeats in nine Women's Super League games.

Having taken seven points from their three European games, the Gunners are at least in a better position in the Women's Champions League, even if their first two performances were far from convincing.

Arsenal required a 93rd-minute penalty to see off Koge on matchday one, before they spurned a goal lead in a frustrating 2-2 draw against Paris FC in the French capital.

The Gunners at least made light work of their home game against Benfica, which saw Alessia Russo net a hat-trick in a commanding 3-0 victory at Meadow Park to become the leading English scorer in UWCL history with 23 goals.

Russo went on to score in a 1-1 draw against Aston Villa before registering a brace in Sunday's 2-1 away win over Liverpool, meaning she has now scored nine goals in Arsenal's last seven competitive matches.

The in-form forward will be hoping to continue her impressive form in Thursday's fixture, as Arsenal look for a repeat of the memorable 1-0 victory they recorded in their most recent meeting with Barcelona in the 2024-25 UWCL final in Lisbon.

Arsenal Women Women's Champions League form:

Barcelona boss Pere Romeu remains without the services of Laia Aleixandri, Kika Nazareth and Vicky Lopez due to injury.

Kerolin has also been added to the injury list, having been ruled out for around four weeks with a calf problem.

Renee van Asten, Patri Guijarro and Aitana Bonmati could all come into the side after dropping down to the bench for the win over Madrid CFF.

As for the visitors, they are expected to be without Mariona Caldentey, Selina Cerci, Katie Reid and Michelle Agyemang, while there are doubts surrounding the availability of Stina Blackstenius and Chloe Kelly.

Defender Taylor Hinds is also an injury concern after being withdrawn in the closing stages of the win over Liverpool.

Former Barcelona full-back Ona Batlle is expected to return to the squad after sitting out Sunday’s game with a knock.

The New Lotus Esprit Supercar Will Be Powered by a 670-HP V-8

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The twin-turbo 4.0-liter engine will pair with a hybrid system, and the duo will make nearly 1000 horsepower combined.

There’s a new V-8-powered Lotus on the way, and we’ve just got our first proper look at the powertrain. While the supercar uses the codename Type 135, most of the world expects it to resurrect the British brand’s legendary Espirt nameplate, and now we know that it’ll have a brand-new 4.0-liter V-8. The engine will team up with a dual-clutch automatic transmission, and both will be built by a company called Horse Powertrain.

The twin-turbocharged 4.0-liter V-8, called the Horse W40, will come in a hot-V configuration, meaning it’ll store its turbos between the cylinder banks, rather than on either side of them. Horse says the combustion half of the powertrain will be responsible for generating 670 horsepower and 590 pound-feet of torque, though it’ll be part of a larger hybrid system that Lotus promises will combine for at least 986 horsepower. With the engine weighing just 441 pounds, that’s quite a power-dense mill.

See all results for new 2029 Lotus Esprit for sale near 43230

Horse also says the engine was designed from the ground up with the hybrid configuration in mind, which required special consideration. Rather than pair it with another company’s transmission, Horse built its own eight-speed dual-clutch automatic called the Horse 8LDCT. The gearbox features a sports car-grade wet dual clutch, which is claimed to enable sub-20-millisecond shift responses, along with a silicon-carbide control system and a variable-displacement mechanical pump.

Horse’s new powertrain components will make their debut on what’s expected to be the upcoming Lotus Esprit. Unfortunately, the car’s production is still some time away, with Lotus planning to reveal and launch the model in 2028.

Jack Fitzgerald’s love for cars stems from his as yet unshakable addiction to Formula 1. After a brief stint as a detailer for a local dealership group in college, he knew he needed a more permanent way to drive all the new cars he couldn’t afford and decided to pursue a career in auto writing. By hounding his college professors at the University of Wisconsin-Milwaukee, he was able to travel Wisconsin seeking out stories in the auto world before landing his dream job at Car and Driver. His new goal is to delay the inevitable demise of his 2010 Volkswagen Golf.

Rothschild Investment Reveals XRP ETF Stake

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Rothschild Investment LLC, the Chicago-based investment management firm, has filed its latest 13F with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), revealing its stake in the Bitwise XRP ETF.

Rothschild Investment’s filing, made on Nov. 10, indicates that the company has invested in 6,300 shares of the Bitwise XRP ETF.

Based on the information provided, these shares were worth $104,769 as of Sept. 30.

The investment in the Bitwise XRP ETF is listed under CUSIP 09174F107 in Rothschild Investment’s third-quarter report.

Interestingly, Rothschild Investment did not have any reported holdings of the XRP ETF in its previous quarterly filing, which indicates that the firm’s investment in the XRP ETF occurred in the third quarter of 2026.

The Bitwise XRP ETF, listed under the ticker XRP, was introduced in November 2025.

The ETF enables traditional investors to invest in the Ripple-linked cryptocurrency without needing to buy and hold XRP tokens themselves.

Although Rothschild Investment has invested in the XRP ETF, it is not a significant part of its overall portfolio.

The firm’s total portfolio value for 13F-reportable holdings is around $2.69 billion.

Rothschild Investment holds much larger stakes in Bitcoin and Ethereum investments.

When it comes to cryptocurrencies, Rothschild Investment’s largest holding is in the iShares Bitcoin Trust ETF by BlackRock, where the company owns 40,188 shares worth around $1.9 million.

Additionally, Rothschild Investment reported holding $911,300 in the Grayscale Bitcoin Trust ETF and $335,160 in the Morgan Stanley Bitcoin Trust ETF.

Other Bitcoin-related investments include the Grayscale Bitcoin Mini Trust ETF ($125,113), VanEck Bitcoin ETF ($118,100), Bitwise Bitcoin ETF ($116,802) and Fidelity Wise Origin Bitcoin Fund ($2,107).

In total, the seven Bitcoin investments represent around $3.51 million in reported holdings.

Rothschild Investment also has stakes in the Grayscale Ethereum Staking ETF worth $538,622 and the Bitwise Ethereum ETF worth $476,500.

The company also has a significant holding in the Bitwise Crypto Industry Innovators ETF, with 24,250 shares worth $615,223.

The firm owns $38,360 worth of shares in the Grayscale CoinDesk Crypto 5 ETF.

Finally, it has invested $69,900 in a Solana ETF.

Aside from cryptocurrency investment funds, Rothschild Investment has shares in publicly traded companies that are heavily involved in the digital asset space.

Michael Saylor’s Strategy is one such holding, with a $530,763 investment.

Other holdings include Coinbase Global ($86,681), Block ($125,060) and Riot Platforms ($42,980), a Bitcoin mining company.

A Kitchen in Bloom: Inside Miele’s Lush Botanical Oasis Where Art Meets Design

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Step inside Art Basel as visionaries Tiffany Howell and Asher Young merge cutting-edge technology with untamed botanical romance, staging an immersive sensory landscape you don’t just see, but feel.

Miele crafts appliances for enduring performance, but they also curate experiences that engage all the senses. Imagine the intuitive precision of a sleek induction cooktop that subtly adjusts temperature on its own; the soft, inviting glow of a French door refrigerator that reveals a curated bounty within ample space; the alluring aroma of a built-in coffee system greeting you each morning; and the calming, almost meditative hum of a steam at work. Each of these choreographed moments elevate Miele appliances beyond a functional tool into something you fundamentally feel. By blending groundbreaking innovation and sustainable features with timeless, minimalist design, Miele frees you to embrace the pure joy of cooking and to savor the intimate, everyday moments that make a house a true home.

For this year’s Art Basel, the ultimate intersection of culture and creativity, Miele brings together two visionaries to redefine the culinary space. Tiffany Howell of Night Palm Design is passionate about creating cinematic homes that tell a deeply personal story. Asher Young is a multidisciplinary artist known for merging art with technology to create immersive spectacle. Together, they have imagined a sensory kitchen built entirely within a lush botanical garden setting. Here, the wildness of the natural world frames the precise German engineering. It is a space where the smooth, handleless silhouettes and cool touch of Miele's Obsidian Black glass stand in striking relief against their organic surroundings, harnessing technology for a design that is both tactile and state-of-the-art. “We used the sleek, matte black Miele appliances as the driving force to bring this to life,” says Howell of the striking visual contrast between nature and cutting-edge machines. “They’re like the perfect little black dress—timeless, elegant, and grounding the entire room.” A deep respect for the environment is also reflected in Miele engineering, which uses innovative technology that preserves ingredients longer—ensuring that the natural world isn't just an aesthetic inspiration, but a core priority for the brand.

Howell’s signature nostalgic romance blends with Asher’s analytical thinking and knack for creating physical, interactive experiences to make the space come alive. Young's touch ensures the installation feels genuinely dynamic, translating the unseen power of Miele’s technology into a captivating physical experience. “How can we create a space that rebalances and also is a place that you can actually engage with? So you can smell the flowers while you're washing your hand. You can feel the cool breeze as you open the fridge,” explains Young. “All these elements is not just for the sake of a visual presentation, but actually to experience and be inside.” The result is an experiential reminder that kitchens should always reflect your personality and passions. “We both said let’s bring our shared language together,” he recalls. The finished design is the undeniable heartbeat of the garden, a space with one foot rooted elegantly in the past and the other stepping boldly into the future.

“A kitchen expresses how you live, what you love and desire, and it’s also where your most cherished memories are made,” says Howell. As the Miami sun sets, the botanical space seamlessly transitions into an elegant, moody enclave for evening entertaining, inviting you to linger just a little bit longer. And in a Miele kitchen, those memories continue into the next day, beginning the moment the fragrant aroma of freshly brewed coffee announces the limitless possibilities of a brand-new morning.

Experience the convergence of culinary art, design, and innovation firsthand. Explore the full Miele collection and begin designing a kitchen that awakens every sense.

Lucid CEO admits it ‘did not even know where cars were’ and lost orders

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Lucid (LCID) CEO Silvio Napoli admitted on the company’s Q3 earnings call that, as recently as this summer, the automaker “did not even know where cars were.” Buyers placed orders, Lucid took up to two weeks to locate and deliver a car, and some of those buyers walked away.

That’s a rough admission from a company sitting on thousands of unsold vehicles. And it’s a problem Tesla ran into, and fixed, back in 2018 at more than ten times Lucid’s current volume.

Napoli made the comment when an analyst asked whether Lucid’s deliveries would go up or down in the fourth quarter. He said that at the start of Q3, the company had orders where it “would take sometimes two weeks to deliver to the customer, and so we would lose the order.”

He said Lucid now has visibility on which cars are being built, where they’re shipped, where they’re stored, and when they can reach a customer. A new commercial team also now sells the cars it knows are available on the day a customer wants one.

Lucid built 3,228 more vehicles than it delivered in the first half of 2026, including 5,500 built but only 3,093 delivered in Q1. Its inventory hit $1.38 billion on the balance sheet at the end of June, which is why we reported earlier this year that Lucid had to adjust production after a bloated Q1 inventory.

So Lucid had thousands of cars on lots and in transit, and it was still losing buyers because it couldn’t match a car to an order fast enough.

In Q3, Lucid cut production to 2,954 vehicles and delivered 3,806, down 7% year over year. Finished goods inventory fell by almost 15% during the quarter, according to new CFO Alexander De Bock. Inventory stood at $1.22 billion on September 30.

Tesla learned this lesson the hard way during the Model 3 ramp.

By Q3 2018, Tesla was making roughly 4,000 Model 3s per week. That’s more cars every week than Lucid delivers in a full quarter today. Deliveries tripled or quadrupled in some markets, and the bottleneck moved from the factory to the trucks.

Elon Musk tweeted in September 2018 that Tesla had “gone from production hell to delivery logistics hell.” A week later, he said Tesla was facing an “extreme shortage of car carrier trailers” and had started building its own car carriers to keep cars moving. Tesla also launched door-to-door delivery and asked existing owners to help at delivery centers.

It didn’t stop there. In November 2018, with the federal tax credit about to be cut in half for Tesla buyers, Musk said Tesla had bought trucking companies and locked in contracts with major haulers. Tesla moved cars east by truck instead of rail to cut weeks off transit times.

Tesla delivered 83,500 vehicles in Q3 2018, including 55,840 Model 3s, a record at the time.

When Model 3 reached Europe in February 2019, the first ship docked at Zeebrugge in Belgium and Tesla immediately had trouble processing the cars at the port.

About a week after the first ship arrived, Tesla ended its contract with the company preparing Model 3s at the port and took over the work itself. The target was 3,000 cars per week coming through one port.

It worked. By March 2019, Tesla delivered more than 5,300 Model 3s in Norway alone, making it the best-selling car in the country by a wide margin. A car that’s sitting at a port or on a lot doesn’t make anyone money. Tesla treated delivery speed as a sales tool, not a back-office job.

We later learned from Musk himself that this “logistic hell” was almost the nail in Tesla’s coffin. The CEO said that Tesla was at the brink of bankruptcy at the time.

This is one of the most embarrassing admissions we’ve heard on an earnings call, and credit to Napoli for saying it out loud. Most executives would have blamed “demand” and moved on.

But it explains a lot. Lucid’s problem has never been the cars. The Air and Gravity are excellent products. The problem has been everything around them: service, software, and now we learn, basic inventory tracking. When you’re spending about $273,000 for every car you deliver, losing a buyer because you can’t find their car two weeks after they ordered is brutal.

Tesla solved this at 4,000 Model 3s a week while fighting for its own survival. Lucid is moving fewer than 300 cars a week. This isn’t a hard problem at that scale. It’s a discipline problem, and Napoli is right to call it that.

If you’re buying an EV, powering it with home solar can cut your fuel costs to nearly zero. With electricity rates up almost 10% last year and expected to keep climbing, going solar is one of the best ways to protect yourself against rising costs. And with lease and PPA options, you can do it with zero upfront cost and start saving immediately. If you want to find the best deal, check out EnergySage. Progressive Overload Training: What It Means, Benefits, And How To Do It, According To Trainers

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Understanding this concept is a must to build muscle, trainers agree.

If you’ve ever Googled “how to gain muscle?” there is no doubt the term “progressive overload” popped up. At its core, progressive overload training is a style of strength training where you gradually increase the amount of stress placed on your body during exercise. But what does that really even mean?

If you want to build bigger muscles, lose weight, and/or lift heavy, you have to gradually place more load and stress on the body to force that initial change and growth in the muscle fiber, explains Kendra Madigan a personal trainer at Physical Equilibrium. Progressive overload training is the way to do that, she adds.

Meet the experts: Kendra Madigan is a dancer and certified personal trainer at Physical Equilibrium. Kate Georgiadis, the founder of LIFT, a New York based fitness initiative.

This article will delve into why progressive overload training works and how to implement it into your routine—along with expert tips on how to prevent injury. Plus, you'll get a four-week full-body training plan designed by Kate Georgiadis, the founder of LIFT, a New York-based fitness initiative focused on empowering women through individual and group training.

With progressive overload training, you want to gradually increase the amount of stress your body experiences during exercise to increase your strength. “This can be achieved by increasing the weight, repetitions, frequency, or intensity of exercises,” says Georgiadis, who explains that this style of training forces the body to adapt to a higher tension than it has previously withstood.

During exercise, your muscles will tear slightly, Madigan says. When the tears heal, it makes the muscle bigger. “So if you’re not lifting heavy enough, you’re not going to get that progressive result that you’re looking for,” she says. However, if you progress too fast and don’t give your muscles time to heal these tiny tears, you risk injury.

So what’s the sweet spot when it comes to increasing weight? For your baseline, you want to begin with a manageable load. “If you think, ‘I can probably do 15 pounds’—start with 10 or 12.5 and make sure you can [manage] that,” says Madigan, “Always start low and go high.”

Next, you want to incorporate incremental load increases. This can look different for everyone, depending on how fast your body is adapting, your experience level, and the frequency you work out.

Increasing the number of reps or volume is one way to bump up your load, but that can become time-consuming as it requires you to lengthen your workout time, so increasing weight tends to be the more common route people take. “For a beginner who starts tracking their weights, the best approach is to keep consistent parameters. Trying to change the reps, weights, and sets each week can be confusing,” says Georgiadis. “I suggest keeping the reps and sets the same over the first four weeks and trying to increase the weight while maintaining proper form.”

To do this, once you can perform your current workout and move your current load with proper form easily, up the weight by 2-5 percent, she recommends. This could be a weekly or biweekly increase, or based on performance and feel.

If you want to go by feel, Madigan recommends doing sets of eight to 12 reps where you are at exhaustion for the last two reps. “You’re going for that exhaustion phase, so that’s how you judge where your weight should be,” she says. Some trainers call this phase “to failure,” but Madigan prefers the word exhaustion. “If you can only do five reps of something, take the weight down a little bit, build your strength until you can get at least 10 reps in, with the last two reps going to exhaustion,” she explains.

Georgiadis gauges when her clients should bump up their weight by having them do a few extra reps. “Every week, I usually push my clients to try to get a few more reps with the weight they are using (do 15 reps instead of 12), and if they can do that, I will increase the weight on the next set or in the next session,” she says.

To help visualize what a progressive overload training schedule would look like, Georgiadis designed the sample full-body plan here. But remember, listen to your body and only increase when you can perform the moves for the full amount of reps with the correct form.

Squat: 3 sets of 8 reps with weight about 5 lbs. heavier than week 1

Dumbbell Chest Press: 3 sets of 8 reps with weight about 2.5 lbs. heavier than week 1

Deadlift: 3 sets of 8 reps with weight about 5 lbs. heavier than week 1

Squat: 3 sets of 8 reps with weight about 5 lbs. heavier than week 2

Dumbbell Chest Press: 3 sets of 8 reps with weight about 2.5 lbs. heavier than week 2

Deadlift: 3 sets of 8 reps with weight about 5 lbs. heavier than week 2

Squat: 3 sets of 9 reps with weight about 10 lbs. heavier than week 1

Dumbbell Chest Press: 3 sets of 9 reps with weight about 5 lbs. heavier than week 1

Deadlift: 3 sets of 9 reps with weight about 10 lbs. heavier than week 1

While you may be tempted to keep pushing to harder and harder resistance every week, it is important to deload during a progressive overload cycle too to prevent overtraining and plateaus. “Deload weeks are periods of reduced training intensity and volume, giving the body time to recover and prepare for more intense training,” says Georgiadis.

She recommends incorporating a deload week every four to eight weeks. She says that she tries to time her clients deload weeks with their vacations so the rest can happen naturally in their routines.

During your fitness journey it can be easy to get stuck in a routine. But if you do the same thing each week you will just maintain your current strength and fitness. In order to improve (get stronger, fitter, lose weight, etc.) you must advance the challenges on your body. Following a progressive overload plan allows you to effectively continue to grow, increasing muscle mass and overall athleticism.

Increasing your intensity too fast is a common cause of injuries, trainers agree. “Allow for rest and recovery, adjusting as needed based on how you feel and perform,” says Georgiadis. “Overtraining can be avoided by listening to your body, ensuring proper nutrition, maintaining hydration, and getting adequate sleep.”

Madigan also says it is important to remember to eat and recover to get the most out of the work you are doing and avoid injury. She explains that when you overtrain, you get more tired and less hungry, “That is just a recipe for an injury,” she says, “So always take the recovery. It’s as important as the workout.”

Beyond taking rest days and listening to your body, there are several other ways to prevent injury while doing a progressive overload style plan. One is by planning your training around your menstrual cycle. The different phases of your cycle can make working out easier and harder, so for some women, a completely linear progressive overload training plan might not work.

“On weeks when hormonal changes make it challenging, it's entirely acceptable not to increase the weight or reps. Instead, you can maintain the current level of intensity and then aim for progression in the following week when you might feel stronger and more capable,” says Georgiadis.

She recommends doing high-intensity workouts and strength training during your follicular phase, doing controlled, linear strength training during ovulation when there is an increased risk of injury, and doing moderate to low intensity exercise during the luteal phase. (Read all about cycle syncing at the link ahead!) “This approach not only respects the body's natural rhythms but also supports sustained progress in training without risking injury or burnout,” she says.

How Casting the New ‘Harry Potter’ Helped ‘Club Kid’ Find Its Adorable 10-Year-Old Star

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Jordan Firstman knew that the success of “Club Kid” hinged on finding the perfect preteen actor to play his character’s long-lost son, Arlo.

It was a demanding role that had a lot of boxes to check off: A British kid, whose hard-partying mother had just committed suicide, finds himself dumped in New York City, where he struggles to break out of his protective shell to bond with his party promoter dad, Peter (Firstman, who wrote and directed the film, in addition to starring in it ). But the perfect candidate had to project an effortless coolness that allows him to organically blend in with his newfound father’s social circle of hard-living, LGBTQ+ clubgoers, whose lives revolve around their love of music, dance, and drugs. Related Stories ‘Victorian Psycho’ Review: Maika Monroe Scares Up a Very Different Kind of Final Girl ‘Steps’ Review: A Disposable Fairy Tale Reminds Us That Cinderella Is Someone’s Hated Stepsister Too

“[Arlo’s] not just cool, he’s empathetic and wise; there’s a lot of stuff about that kid that was on the page that needed to be there,” said Firstman, who on this week’s episode of the Filmmaker Toolkit Podcast, called the casting search the most “nerve-wracking” part of making “Club Kid.”

“Club Kid” had the right casting director for the job. Carmen Cuba has a legendary track record of finding child stars, including the original “Stranger Things” ensemble. But as Cuba pointed out while participating in IndieWire’s Craft Roundtable of Casting Directors, “Club Kid” was different from those previous assignments.

“I do six-month searches for kids,” said Cuba. “On ’Stranger Things,’ ‘Starfighter,’ you have time and money, and this was a small film.”

The $4 to $6 million production (reports on “Club Kid’s” budget have varied; A24 did not reply to IndieWire’s inquiry) could not afford a lengthy search. It didn’t even have the money for Cuba to fly to London with Firstman for auditions. She would be reliant on a UK casting team, working within a limited budget, to help her find a needle in a haystack.

“I was very nervous about just sort of the plan of attack, because I was going to have a UK team, and you need a British kid who looks like he’s half-Jordan,” said Cuba.

Right before jumping into “Club Kid,” Cuba went on her yearly vacation with a group of close casting director friends. As the holiday wound down, Cuba tried to work through her upcoming casting dilemma with some of her closest colleagues.

“I [said], ‘How am I going do this?’ just trying to map it out for myself before I left to go start it,” recalled Cuba of the conversation. “And one of those friends is Lucy Bevan, who had just finished her ‘Harry Potter’ search, so she so generously said, ‘Why don’t I share some of the names.’”

To cast the first season of the $200 million-plus upcoming HBO series — based on the first Harry Potter book, “The Philosopher’s Stone,” in which Harry turns 11 — Bevans had just finished her own six-month search. She had auditioned thousands of young British actors to play the next generation of Harry, Hermione, Ron, and their Hogwarts classmates.

“This is just a testament to our relationship because Lucy would not normally do UK casting on a tiny movie,” said Cuba of the prominent British casting director, whose credits include “Barbie,” “F1,” and “The Batman.”

“She had just seen 40,000 kids,” said Firstman of Beaven, who early on showed him a photo of Reggie Absolom. “I saw his face and I was like, ‘that is the face that I’ve seen in my head.’ Even in the script, he’s supposed to be a quarter Indian – “Innit Babes” [Peter and his friends’ nickname for Reggie’s mom based on her favorite expression] is supposed to be like half Indian — and Lucy [said], ‘Let’s not focus on that. That’s not going happen.’ And Reggie’s a quarter Indian. Every single thing that I had asked God for, this kid came to me in Reggie.”

In total, according to Cuba, Bevan showed Firstman 90 kids, with a wider range of ages — Cuba and Firstman went back and forth about how old Reggie should be, with the character originally skewing younger than 10 — but Firstman never wavered from Absalom, whose résumé included roles in “Silo” and “The Other Bennet Sister.”

“Lucy was so generous. They had no money to fly me to London to do the session in person, so she hosted the callback session there,” said Cuba. “Jordan is incredible with kids, he was like, ‘Let’s not do it in this casting office, let’s take it outside.’ So suddenly Lucy’s assistant is outside with the camera, and they’re walking and talking. I rewatched that audition recently, and it’s just amazing. Jordan’s brain, and how he connects to everyone that he casts. I think that’s what you feel in the movie; it’s so familial.”

On the podcast, Firstman talked about how beyond his decade of drug-fueled clubbing in Europe and New York City, one of the inspirations for “Club Kid” was the bond he formed with one of his close friend’s child, something he felt would be essential to replicate with Absolom.

“The reason kids like me is I don’t treat them as kids,” said Firstman. “They’re surprised at my candor, or the inappropriate things that most adults wouldn’t be willing to say in front of a kid. I know what Reggie sees on the internet. I’m not going to say something too much, but I can push it a little bit.  I think he always appreciated that in me, and once he understood that I liked to be made fun of too, everything shifted. Once he got permission to make fun of me, the whole dynamic shifted, and we just got so much more comfortable around each other.”

Much like his unorthodox, take-it-outside, walk-and-talk callback audition with Absolom, Firstman quickly realized, after casting the young actor, traditional preparation wouldn’t work.

“We tried doing traditional rehearsals, and it was awkward,” said Firstman. “I [said], ‘Let’s just keep hanging out, and keep having pizza together, and keep talking about music, and keep talking about TikTok clips we’ve seen, and send each other TikToks,’ and that’s how we bonded.”

An A24 release, “Club Kid” is now in New York City and Los Angeles theaters, and will expand on Friday, November 13.

Conor Oberst Will Play a Cave and a Haunted Hotel on His First Solo Tour Since 2019

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Conor Oberst has announced a 2027 North American tour, which will mark his first solo headlining trek in eight years.

The 49-date “An Alleged Evening With Conor Oberst” tour will find the singer-songwriter traveling around the West Coast, Midwest, and the South. (While there aren’t any northeast dates on the itinerary now, more shows are expected to be announced.) The trek will kick off  Feb. 19 at Liberty Hall in Lawrence, Kansas, and will wrap with two nights, May 14 and 15, at the Stanley Hotel in Estes Park, Colorado.

While Oberst will primarily play theaters on the tour, there are some particularly unique shows on the schedule. The Stanley Hotel, for instance, is the famous haunted hotel that inspired The Shining. On April 22, Oberst will play La Rosa, a converted Benedictine monastery in Tucson, Arizona on April 22. And on March 19, Oberst will perform at the Caverns, a literal cave in Pelham, Tennessee.

Tickets for all shows will go on sale this Friday, Nov. 13, starting at 10 a.m. local time. Full information is available on Oberst’s website.

“An Alleged Evening With Conor Oberst” will be the singer-songwriter’s first solo headlining tour since 2019, when he was still promoting his most recent solo LP, 2017’s Salutations. (That year, he also toured extensively with Phoebe Bridgers as Better Oblivion Community Center.) After that, Oberst launched a run of residency-style shows in 2024, while also playing some shows with Bright Eyes. Why United Airlines Accepts A 222-Seat Jet Over A Standard 257-Seat One On Its CES Tokyo Route

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

In January 2027, United Airlines will begin new nonstop flights between Tokyo Narita International Airport (NRT) and Las Vegas Harry Reid International Airport (LAS). This is an unusual launch on the surface, since LAS is not a major hub for the airline, but it is a limited service scheduled around the annual Consumer Electronics Show (CES), held from January 6 to January 9. The carrier will run three NRT-LAS flights on January 3, 4, and 5, and three LAS-NRT nonstop flights on January 9, 10, and 11.

The three NRT-LAS flights before the 2027 CES will use United's Boeing 777-200ER, featuring 276 seats and 50 Polaris seats. The three flights to Tokyo, however, will use the Boeing 787-9 rather than the 777. More interestingly, the airline will use its new Elevated 787-9 with its latest interiors and a premium-heavy configuration, featuring 64 Polaris suites and just 222 seats in total. It's unusual to use two different aircraft on the same route in opposite directions, especially when one has 54 more seats than the other.

While LAS is a large spoke for United, it's not a particularly high-priority market, and the CES services exist to better serve existing Star Alliance customers who need to travel from Asia to CES nonstop, helping retain customer loyalty. In addition, these services also help expand United's and All Nippon Airways' (the two operate a metal-neutral transpacific joint venture) potential customer base, as some customers may have booked away from the two in the past for CES due to the lack of a nonstop.

These routes work because of the sudden surge in demand to Las Vegas during this period, but they won't operate for a long period of time, which means United needs to find extra aircraft capable of operating the route. The 777 is coming from San Francisco, with United scheduling a second daily flight from San Francisco to NRT under the flight number UA 936 on January 2, 3, and 4. In NRT, the 777 turns around for UA 937 to LAS; after arrival, it operates a service to San Francisco again.

The return leg with the 787-9 works slightly differently. United has not scheduled a domestic leg to LAS with the Elevated 787-9, which means that the aircraft will need to be ferried. It then operates UA 7 to NRT, turns around, then operates UA 6 to IAH. Crucially, UA 7 is ordinarily used for the airline's outbound service from IAH to NRT, but this flight simply won't operate on the three days when the LAS-NRT flights are running. In contrast, United is adding a second 777 flight from SFO to NRT to position the aircraft for the NRT-LAS flight.

With these services, United is essentially using spare aircraft capacity, with the 777-200ER fleet having enough slack during those three days to operate a SFO-NRT-LAS-SFO rotation. Meanwhile, the airline felt that it was lucrative to cancel the outbound IAH-NRT leg for three days and use the Elevated 787-9 from LAS to NRT. Importantly, these decisions are being made based on what aircraft is available, not necessarily because the carrier needs to fly the 777-200ER or Elevated 787-9 on this service.

Every business decision comes with an opportunity cost. In the case of the LAS-NRT flight, United chose to cancel the IAH-NRT flight for three days and use that aircraft to operate the LAS-NRT flight, a decision weighed against pulling an aircraft from elsewhere. This doesn't necessarily mean that the IAH-NRT flight is particularly weak, but the math favored this solution above all others. In addition, the Elevated 787-9 could also be particularly desirable for this route.

The Elevated Interior and 222-seat layout debuted only in 2026, while January 2027 will be the first time United operates CES services. Primarily, these flights are about increasing customer loyalty and expanding the customer base, so United is putting its latest and greatest aircraft on this route. The Elevated 787-9 not only has the airline's newest seats, but it also has an extremely premium-heavy layout, and exposing these customers to the best United can offer further supports its mission.

Decisions like these involve multiple factors, including the IAH-NRT flight's performance on the selected days, the value of flying the Elevated 787-9 on LAS-NRT, the opportunity cost of pulling an aircraft from another route, and availability. It's also worth noting that mid-January generally has low travel demand in the US, which may explain why United could source a spare 777 and why United may have been inclined to cancel the IAH-NRT flight for three days. Of course, the airline has not confirmed these reasons.

Delta One and United Polaris are leading business class products, but which one is the better choice? Is network coverage better than luxury?

The Elevated 787-9 has significantly fewer seats than United's standard 787-9, but it has substantially more premium capacity. For the customer base booking flights for CES, the added Polaris and Premium Plus capacity is exactly what is desired, which means that yields will likely remain high while most of the seats will still be filled. As such, the carrier should earn a substantial amount of revenue from operating this route with the Elevated 787-9, even if economy capacity is low.

Although the Elevated 787-9 has significantly more premium seats than the Boeing 777-200ER, the 777 is still a very premium-heavy aircraft. With 50 Polaris seats and 24 Premium Plus seats, the 777-200ER has more premium capacity than the standard 787-9, and it is one of the most premium-heavy aircraft in United's fleet. The use of the 777-200ER demonstrates that the airline clearly saw strong premium demand, even if the return flight uses an even more premium-heavy aircraft.

Offering the Elevated 787-9 on this route may have been United's priority, but the aircraft may not have been available to pull from another service in both directions. This could be why the airline instead pulled a 777-200ER for the flights to LAS, but then took an Elevated 787-9 from the Houston route on days when demand may have been weaker. Because of the limited nature of this route, opportunity cost plays a much bigger factor.

United currently has 51 internationally configured 777-200ERs, while only 16 of its 64 787-9s are in the 222-seat layout with the Elevated Interior. There is a significant mismatch in capacity between the two segments, while premium capacity is actually much higher on the 787. United installs 50 Polaris seats, 24 Premium Plus seats, 46 Economy Plus seats, and 156 standard economy seats on the 777-200ER. Meanwhile, the Elevated 787-9 is equipped with 64 Polaris Suites, 35 Premium Plus seats, 39 Economy Plus seats, and just 84 economy seats.

Inside, the Elevated 787s feature the next generation of Polaris business class seats. Based on the Elevate Ascent, these seats are configured in a reverse-herringbone layout while featuring sliding doors. The screens are larger than the prior generation of Polaris seats, and the airline has configured half of the center seats in a herringbone pattern, creating honeymoon suites that can also partially convert into a double bed. Furthermore, these planes feature front-row 'Polaris Studio' suites.

United has also invested in the soft product of its cabins, including introducing new service features and amenities for the Polaris cabins. Meanwhile, the rest of the aircraft has been thoroughly updated compared to the airline's prior-generation widebody interiors. Premium Plus now offers headrest dividers for increased privacy, larger screens, new charging options, a water bottle holder, and a built-in reading light. Meanwhile, the economy seats come with larger screens and Bluetooth connectivity.

United's new premium-heavy layout on its Boeing 787-9 fleet is designed to compete with the world's best airlines.

American Airlines has long operated January-only transpacific services from Las Vegas for CES. In 2027, the airline will operate nonstop flights from LAS to NRT and Seoul Incheon International Airport (ICN), using a 273-seat 777-200ER on both flights in both directions. As with United's CES flights, American's service will operate only for a limited time, although it is significantly larger than United in this market.

While American is the more established player on CES routes from Asia, Delta Air Lines is growing quickly. In 2027, the carrier will run nonstop flights between LAS and ICN, Hong Kong International Airport (HKG), Shanghai Pudong International Airport (PVG), and Taipei Taoyuan International Airport (TPE). In addition, Delta is running nonstop flights to London Heathrow Airport (LHR), competing against American Airlines on this route, while also operating flights to Paris Charles de Gaulle Airport (CDG) and Amsterdam Schiphol Airport (AMS).

These services are all limited, although United is undoubtedly the small newcomer to this market. Overall, this is a small but lucrative market that US airlines are increasingly interested in, but only for a very short period.

4 Upcoming Fantasy RPGs Better Than Waiting for a New Dragon Age

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The fire’s burning low. The road ahead looks grim. The companion who has spent half your journey needling you clearly has something on their mind, and you’re one or two correct dialogue choices away from getting them to spill. You pick a reply. You feel it land. Then somebody makes a joke about cheese, and the tension breaks.

This is one of the many things the Dragon Age franchise does well. In this series, from Origins to Veilguard, you gather a party of real-feeling companions and grapple with party management. These are also tactical games — games where you need to pause your combat encounters, point at the biggest threat, and hope against hope that your mage remembers she’s standing in a pool of fire. They’re political games, full of nobles and templars quibbling about the fates of their respective territories and teams. And they’re games where consequences stick. A choice you made in a prologue will come back to bite you hours (or entire games) later, usually while you’re just minding your own business collecting elfroot for a salve.

It may be unlikely that we’ll see a new Dragon Age title anytime soon. (Earlier this month, former Dragon Age writer David Gaider said that “the golden days are over.”) Yet there are still campfires worth stopping at. If you miss that feeling of saving Thedas by day and hanging out with your party of oddball companions by night, check out these four upcoming RPGs. Consider this your recruitment screen.

You can never keep every companion happy in Dragon Age. Solasta 2 eases you into this never-ending quest for balance by starting you off with your family. You lead the Colwall siblings, orphans from a household tied to a religious order sworn against an evil called Shadwyn. Soon, the siblings travel to a new continent, where the opening city, Caer Mar, comes with rebels, factions, and plenty of trouble.

If you liked pausing Dragon Age to plan every fight, this turn-based tactical RPG (which has been in Steam Early Access since March 2026) offers plenty of occasions to plan. Positioning, line of sight, and high ground decide most battles, and D&D fans will appreciate the chance to use spells like Magic Missile and Shatter to deal damage. Between fights, as you trek your way across Neokos, your role-playing choices will influence your standing with the world’s factions — which, in turn, changes how the world responds to you. Cross the wrong faction, and the merchants may stop selling you the good stuff.

Solasta 2’s Early Access only covers part of Act 1 and comes with a low level cap — you can only get to level 4 at this time. Developer Tactical Adventures says it intends to stay in Early Access for about a year, and 1.0 will raise the cap to 10 and finish the main questline. While you wait, you can play the first game, Solasta: Crown of the Magister. It’s a crunchier D&D experience, so brush up on your spell strategy before you boot it up.

What if the Dragon Age: Origins prologue cast you as a vagrant with a bad attitude and no plan? In Outward 2, you play an ordinary person in Aurai, a dangerous world where most people never leave their hometown walls. You’ll choose one of three starting scenarios — a miner in the northern mountains of Tramontane, a homeowner in the underground city of Haboob, or a vagrant in Simeon’s Bastion. Then, you’ll get a background and personality traits like Hoarder, Gourmet, or Lone Wolf that change your dialogue, starting gear, and aptitudes.

Surviving Aurai will take more than a sword. Hunger, thirst, exhaustion, and weather threaten you as much as the world’s creatures do…and you don’t get mounts or fast travel to give you a quick way out of a tight bind. This game is more committed to realism (read: suffering) than your usual RPG. Armor weight will change how fast you move. If you want to stay mobile during a fight, you’ll need to drop your backpack. You can wield a different weapon in each hand, like a sword and shield, dual axes, or a mace and pistol, and trainers teach skills for a fee. But you can’t just be focused on survival: Your choices will pull you toward a faction that will shape your story. Will you join a budding mercenary company, a society of historians, or a humanitarian mission? What’ll happen after you do?

Outward 2 will support online and split-screen co-op, which means your companions can also be your IRL friends. While you wait for this title, play the first Outward if you like your fantasy with a side of hypothermia. Regardless, bring a friend. And a warm cloak, because the seasons in Aurai show no mercy.

Release date: Feb. 23, 2027 (Feb. 18 with the Premium Edition)

Where to play: PS5, Windows PC, Xbox Series X

This game’s not out yet, but Albion already has opinions about you. Here, you play as the hero of Briar Hill, “an ordinary person made extraordinary by fate.” From there, it’s up to you to decide what kind of hero you’ll be. Your built-in rival is Isabel, played by Hayley Atwell, a driven, powerful hero on a quest to right a tragic injustice. She opposes you and Humphry, her former guardian. With interpersonal conflict this charged, who needs a world to save?

Dragon Age taught us that the world should remember what you did, and Albion takes the lesson seriously. Your reputation affects how the world’s 1,000+ handcrafted NPCs treat you. That reputation can reshape the world around you and grow your power. Beyond reputation, you can romance villagers, have children with them, and start your own cozy life while chaos reigns above you.

Dragon Age blends dark subject matter with humor very well. Fable seems ready to carry on that particular mission. One of its spells, “Befowl,” turns enemies into chickens (and undead enemies into undead chickens, naturally). Isabel has an injustice to right and you’re standing in her way, so keep that chicken spell handy.

A weekly cheat sheet to what you’ll eventually love to play. No buying, no commitment — just saving for later.

If you ever wished you could replay your first Dragon Age campaign and fix just one decision, Clockwork Revolution might be your invitation to do just that. Dragon Age gave us choices that follow you from game to game. InXile’s first-person RPG explicitly lets you rewrite your own decisions to answer the question: Would anything change if you could reverse something from your past?

You play Morgan Vanette, a customizable member of the Rotten Row Hooligans gang in Avalon, a steampunk city whose factions run from the slums to the golden mansions. The villain, Lady Ironwood, already rules Avalon, and she’s been using time travel to keep it that way. Vanette and a band of allies set out to turn back the clock and right her wrongs.

Sounds standard, right? The twist, here, is how you go about doing that. You’ll use a device called the Chronometer to change key moments in the past. This sounds excellent in terms of instant gratification, but the effects of those changes threaten to ripple outward. The people closest to you feel it first, and the crew you return to after time shenanigans may differ from the one you left. Your allies, Ulysses, Nazim, Erasmus, Hazel, and Anne, have lives of their own, with stories that don’t revolve around you. If you enjoy political chess, Lady Ironwood gives you a rival to follow. A flying automaton named Prentice will guide you through the branching realities. You’ll build a character with attributes, skills, and gear, hack hostile automatons, and use an ability called Displace to toss an explosive barrel at enemies. Whatever you do, just change the past carefully, because your crew will remember.

A distant exoplanet mysteriously vanished from sight. Astronomers just discovered why

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

"We think of the universe as resembling clockwork, where everything is regular, but that's not true."

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Astronomers have solved the mystery of an extrasolar planet, or exoplanet, that "disappeared" after seven years of observations. New research suggests that the planet hasn't vanished at all, but has been dragged off its orbit by an unseen planet.

The exoplanet, designated TOI-2535 b, is located around 1,000 light-years away. TOI-2535 b was routinely observed with NASA's Transiting Exoplanet Survey Satellite (TESS) space telescope for seven years between April 2019 and March 2026 as it completed its 8.25-Earth day orbit of its parent star. That's 10 times quicker than the orbit of Mercury around the sun and means this blisteringly hot planet is classed as a "hot Jupiter."

However, something strange happened started to happen as astronomers observed the distant world. The orbits of the planet weren't regular like those of other planets, with TOI-2535 b often arriving six hours early or six hours later than when astronomers expected. Then, in March this year, TESS was no longer spotting the characteristic drops in starlight that TOI-2535 b caused as it crossed, or "transited," the face of its parent star. It seemed to have vanished.

This team used TESS data to determine that TOI-2535 b is being pulled on by an unseen neighbor and is, in turn, pulling on that planet as well. This is causing the orbits of both worlds in the TOI-2535 system to "wobble." TOI-2535 b disappeared when that wobble tilted it out of alignment with its star.

"We think of the universe as resembling clockwork, where everything is regular, but that's not true. If there are multiple planets in a system, they can affect each other's orbits. We know of a few systems that are like this," team member Samuel Yee of the University of California, Los Angeles, said in a statement. "This is the most extreme example we've seen because the two planets are interacting so strongly. The planet's orbit is tilting so quickly that its transit disappeared while we were watching."

TOI-2535 b isn't expected to transit its parent star for at least 30 years, but the hidden neighbor tugging on it may cross the face of the star sooner than that.

In the meantime, scientists will attempt to decode what this extreme wobbling exoplanet system says about how it formed.

There is currently a lot of speculation about how hot Jupiter planets like TOI-2535 b form. That is because current routes of planet formation suggest planets like this would need to form around a great deal of ice. Obviously, ice isn't likely to be abundant as close to a star as TOI-2535 b is currently.

This means that hot Jupiters likely form far from their stars and then migrate inwards. There are two ways that this could happen. The planet could be excited into an elliptical orbit by violent events like a near-collision with another planetary object, and then interactions with the star pull the planet inwards.

Alternatively, the hot Jupiter may be pulled inwards along with the other planets while sitting in a flattened cloud of gas and dust around the infant star called a protoplanetary disk.

If this second, less turbulent route occurs to bring hot Jupiters close to their stars, then the resultant system should be very flat. In other words, there shouldn't be extreme tilts between the orbits of the planets in that system.

However, the tilt caused to TOI-2535 b by its unseen companion indicates that flattened systems can be twisted by more tilted orbits later in their existence. This makes the slower migration possible despite the orbital tilt.

"Because TOI-2535 b has a close neighbor, it probably migrated by slowly spiraling in instead of getting flung in," Yee said. "We may be seeing a formation channel that doesn’t happen very often but is very interesting."

The team's research was published on Monday (Nov. 9) in the Astrophysical Journal Letters.

Robert Lea is a science journalist in the U.K. whose articles have been published in Physics World, New Scientist, Astronomy Magazine, All About Space, Newsweek and ZME Science. He also writes about science communication for Elsevier and the European Journal of Physics. Rob holds a bachelor of science degree in physics and astronomy from the U.K.’s Open University. Follow him on Twitter @sciencef1rst.

Shaw Contract Design Awards name top interiors of 2026

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

A Japanese office intended to evoke a tree canopy, and a revived US concert hall are among the interior design projects to win Best of Globe at this year’s Shaw Contract Design Awards.

The upgrade of an athletics clubhouse in the US, a legal workplace in the UK and a large three-story office in India also took the top prize in the annual award programme, now in its 21st year.

Held annually by flooring manufacturer Shaw Contract, the Shaw Contract Design Awards celebrate interior design projects that “spark creativity, connect communities and contribute to a better, more sustainable world”.

This year’s winning projects were selected from nearly 550 global submissions by an international jury of architects and designers, with interior design students also joining as part of Shaw’s commitment to supporting the next generation of design talent.

According to the jury, the 2026 winners were unified by “a clear design narrative, an understanding of local context, varied and inclusive user experiences, responsible material strategies and a meaningful relationship between design intent and completed space” – despite their varying scales and styles.

“Throughout the global judging session, the jury returned to a central question: whether each project’s stated intent was visible and understandable in the completed design,” explained Shaw Contract.

“The panel distinguished between attractive spaces and projects that used planning, materials, detail and experience to communicate a clear and consequential idea.”

The St Louis Symphony Orchestra Powell Hall in the US, overhauled by Christner Architects, was one of the winners.

According to Shaw Contract, the jury selected it for its sensitive balance of old and new elements, as well as the important social role it plays in the city, thanks to improved accessibility and audience facilities.

Also in the US is the winning Missouri Athletic Club project, completed by HOK. The scheme involved revamping an existing clubhouse to better suit the needs of its current members.

HOK’s design was commended for adding “a new layer to the club’s heritage rather than treating the past as a fixed visual theme”, according to the brand, introducing a contemporary layout and aesthetic.

The third winner was We Labo 25F, a small workspace in Japan that embodies the principles of biophilic design – the use of natural features such as plants to improve the wellbeing of building occupants.

Its architect, Okamura, was praised for incorporating greenery as more than just “a decorative layer” and creating an interior that mimics working beneath a tree canopy.

The office was also selected in recognition of its adaptable interiors and circular design features, including furniture made from tsunami debris.

We Labo 25F was one of three workplaces to win Best of Globe, alongside Diageo by M Moser Associates in India and DLA Piper by Sheppard Robson Architects in the UK.

Diageo spans 7,700 square metres and accommodates 300 employees across each of its three floors, accommodating its owner’s expansion from Bengaluru to Gurugram.

It was selected by the jury for its “richly layered and carefully crafted” interiors, which combine a wide range of workspaces with shared hospitality areas. M Moser Associates dedicated more than half of the building to community spaces.

Finally, DLA Piper by Sheppard Robson Architects is a large workplace for a legal company. It was designed to cater to different work styles.

According to Shaw Contract, it won the prize for providing a broad range of workspaces suited to different needs, as well as wellbeing, dining and meeting areas.

The jury particularly enjoyed how materials had been applied in a way that adds “warmth and richness into areas that might traditionally be treated as purely corporate”.

The Best of Globe winners are selected from the programme’s Best of Region winners. Winners receive global media exposure and a donation to a charity of their choice.

Shaw Contract’s ambition for the award programme is to encourage discourse within the international creative community.

To learn more about Shaw Contract and the awards programme, visit its website here.

MARA Sells 13,597 Bitcoin for $1.1B as Its AI Pivot Reshapes Treasury Strategy

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Strict editorial policy that focuses on accuracy, relevance, and impartiality

Morbi pretium leo et nisl aliquam mollis. Quisque arcu lorem, ultricies quis pellentesque nec, ullamcorper eu odio.

MARA has put a sizeable number on the changing economics of Bitcoin mining. In its third-quarter shareholder letter, the company said it sold 13,597 BTC after September 30 for approximately $1.1 billion, at an average selling price of about $81,039. It used $750 million of the proceeds to repay Bitcoin-collateralized loans, releasing 17,477 BTC that had been pledged as security.

TL;DR: MARA disclosed $1.1 billion of post-quarter Bitcoin sales and repayment of $750 million in BTC-backed borrowing. The miner is increasingly monetizing its coin reserves to support AI infrastructure and other capital priorities. This marks a meaningful departure from its earlier strategy of holding mined Bitcoin.

At the end of September, MARA reported 34,012 BTC in total holdings, including a sizeable portion lent out or pledged as collateral. The newly disclosed disposals followed quarter-end, so that September balance should not be presented as the company’s current holding without adjustment. MARA said the repayments reduced the proportion of Bitcoin lent or pledged to less than 5% of its remaining holdings, versus roughly 57% at the earlier reporting date.

Management describes the move as a way to gain financial flexibility while still using Bitcoin strategically. Borrowing against the coins had helped finance the proposed acquisition of the Long Ridge natural-gas power business. The subsequent sale allowed MARA to clear the borrowing before the transaction closed, instead of leaving its financing exposed to future collateral calls if Bitcoin weakened.

That is a trade-off, not a free improvement. Selling reserve assets reduces direct exposure to any subsequent Bitcoin recovery. Keeping more BTC would preserve that upside but could require accepting additional liquidity and borrowing risk. The appropriate decision depends on the company’s priorities and its ability to earn a return on the infrastructure being funded.

MARA’s third-quarter revenue fell about 35% from a year earlier to $164.2 million. Its mining operations still produce coins, but the company is now emphasizing power assets and potential data-center customers alongside hashrate. Long Ridge includes generating capacity and land that could support a data-center campus. Development plans are not the same as signed, revenue-producing leases.

The move also fits a wider sector pattern. NewsBTC recently reported how Riot sold more than 20,000 BTC while pursuing AI-related data-center agreements. The contrast with Strive’s rapid Bitcoin accumulation is useful: public companies can use the same underlying asset in radically different corporate strategies.

MARA also disclosed authorization for up to $500 million in share repurchases, while its reported quarterly profit included substantial accounting effects linked to digital assets. Those items should not be mistaken for recurring mining cash flow. Nor does an authorization guarantee that every dollar of the program will be spent.

The follow-up questions are whether Long Ridge closes, whether planned data-center leases are signed, how much Bitcoin remains unrestricted, and whether new AI revenue eventually justifies the capital committed. The BTC sale is confirmed. The return on the assets it may finance is still an execution story.

Sony Cancels Fairgames After Years of Work on PS5 Live Service Shooter

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

PlayStation has officially cancelled Fairgames, the PS5 and PC live service shooter it had in production at Haven Studio.

The news comes from Bloomberg journalist Jason Schreier, though it's been sourced from an internal email distributed to PlayStation employees, and a company spokesperson has commented on the story.

That spokesperson told the outlet that Haven Studio will remain open following the cancellation, and it won't suffer any job losses. The developer, originally headed by Jade Raymond of Assassin's Creed fame — who left about a year and a half ago — will now become a support team within PlayStation Studios.

In the email to PlayStation employees, it's said a recent test of Fairgames didn't deliver the results the company wanted.

It said: "While the test demonstrated strong operational excellence, live ops readiness and community engagement, player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set. Combined with challenging market conditions in the PVP shooter genre, we have made the difficult decision to cease development on the game."

Announced more than three years ago, Fairgames was one of the headline announcements from the infamous PlayStation Showcase that properly pushed Sony's live service efforts — it was also where Concord and Helldivers 2 debuted.

Since then, absolutely nothing official from the game has been made public except for studio departures.

The game has reportedly done poorly during pre-launch and private tests, which has now been confirmed in the email to employees this week. There were rumours Fairgames would be brought back with a new name, Break In, around the summer season of games conferences earlier this year, but it never materialised.

It's yet another failure from PlayStation's first-party efforts during the PS5 era, which has seen many cancellations to the likes of The Last of Us Online, Payback from Bungie, live service titles from Sony Bend and Bluepoint Games, and Twisted Metal. You might as well throw Concord in there too, because while it did actually release, it was taken offline permanently after just two weeks.

Let's hope the PS6 generation doesn't follow in these footsteps.
