What is the 'Dead Internet Theory' and should humanity be worried?

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

When spending time online today, do you ever get the feeling that digital life has become eerily less human? Perhaps social media posts seem a bit off, or you get the sneaking suspicion the customer service representative you are chatting with is actually a bot. If this sounds familiar, you aren't alone: a paradigm called the "dead internet theory" has been gaining momentum in the last several years, positing that the internet is increasingly dominated by non-human activity, including bots and AI-generated content.

Since the theory first started making the rounds on internet forums in the 2010s, AI has only gotten more powerful. In September 2026, OpenAI reported that it had found a solution to a famously difficult mathematics puzzle, the Navier-Stokes problem. Meanwhile, a 2026 report from cybersecurity group Thales found that automated traffic continues to overtake human activity online, with bots accounting for 53 percent of all web traffic, up from 51 percent the year before. And bots are now talking to each other without humans in the loop at all: In March, Meta acquired Moltbook — a social network on which AI chatbots communicate with one another, often discussing humans. So as the internet landscape potentially gets less human every day, what exactly is dead internet theory, where does it come from, and should we be worried?

Dead Internet Theory — the idea that the majority of the internet is propelled by bots and other non-human agents – has been floating around for quite some time. It first emerged as a conspiracy theory on internet forums in the late 2010s, but gained a more concrete framework following a post in 2021 on online community Agora Road titled, "Dead Internet Theory: Most of the Internet Is Fake," which argued "the Internet feels empty and devoid of people." Followers of the theory argued that the internet "died" around 2016 when search engines and social media feeds started surfacing more AI-generated content.

The popularity of chatbots and AI-generated content has only further expanded since then. In 2024, only 33 percent of Americans said they had used chatbots like ChatGPT, Gemini or Copilot, a study from Pew Research found. By 2026, that number had increased to nearly half of Americans at 49 percent. That same report found that 25 percent of Americans use chatbots every day. In many realms of society we are now facing chatbot loops: in schools, students are writing essays using AI, which teachers then grade with AI. In the job search, employees use chatbots to spit out optimized resumes and cover letters which are then analyzed by AI systems. Increasingly, humans are cut out of the conversation entirely, our voices replaced with bots.

On a personal level, this chatbot loop can feel uncanny. Those script-like posts we see on LinkedIn or a soulless AI-generated poster at your local cafe can induce a feeling of existential dread, but the consequences go beyond personal discomfort. Bots can spread disinformation that causes real harm. AI chatbots are increasingly a source of election disinformation, and little is being done legislatively to regulate this new frontier, though government agencies have issued warnings on deepfakes and other AI-powered risks to free elections.

As AI use grows, so has the backlash: 40 percent of Americans say they think AI will have a negative impact on society in the next 20 years, according to a Pew Research study. AI detection is becoming increasingly popular, with company Pangram receiving $9 million in backing in July 2026 for its AI detection system. Following the passage of the EU AI Act, OpenAI has started watermarking AI-generated content. With measures like these, people opposed to AI will hopefully be able to more easily spot and avoid the artificial slop in favor of the human-centric connection they initially came to the internet to find.

Henry Rollins’ Latest Chapter? Punk Rock Archivist

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Inside a moderately-sized building in Nashville’s gentrifying-too-fast Wedgewood-Houston neighborhood, Henry Rollins confirms the rumblings that have been circulating in town: He is in fact a Music City resident. And he’s intent on giving back.

Over two days last weekend, Rollins unveiled “Cool Evidence: Captured Moments From the Last Century,” a curated exhibit featuring select items from his personal punk-rock history collection. Free to the public, the event at the venue Radar615 drew lines a few hundred people deep, each of them eager to lay eyes on relics from a niche but impactful musical past. There were gig posters for Minor Threat, rare photographs of Iggy Pop, movie posters that inspired the Misfits, given to him by Glenn Danzig, and the Häagen-Dazs shirt that Rollins himself wore at his last straight job slinging ice cream.

“I guard all of this very jealously,” Rollins tells Rolling Stone during a friends, family, and media preview the day before the public opening. He says he wanted “Cool Evidence” to feel like a 15-year-old boy’s bedroom wall meets the Smithsonian, and he hoped it would overwhelm guests, leaving them disoriented as they walked away.

Rollins moved to Nashville five years ago, after four decades in Los Angeles, and initially bought a building a few blocks from Radar615 to house his collection and serve as de facto Rollins HQ. But after trying to navigate bureaucratic red tape, and at his accountant’s urging, he sold it. That building has since been demolished, but Rollins’ exhibit teems with punk-rock energy.

The Black Flag singer and leader of Rollins Band developed an appreciation for collecting as a teen in Washington, D.C., with his friend Ian MacKaye, future singer for Minor Threat/Fugazi and co-proprietor of Dischord Records. “I’m not trying to mythologize myself, but we’re these two last good men standing, in that we don’t lie. We don’t rip you off,” Rollins, clad in a black shirt, black Dickies work pants, and white On Cloud sneakers, says. “We can finish a sentence because we never got high, and we’re not dead yet.” Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

While putting up flyers as a teen for MacKaye’s band Teen Idles, they witnessed someone tear one down. Rollins says he felt as if the vandal was “trying to erase our history,” so he started keeping three of everything: copied cassette tapes, Bad Brains flyers, set lists. “What, you’re not going to take that?” he asks, incredulously.

As his reputation as a historian grew, Rollins, who describes himself as a “stuff person,” became the archivist for others, including the Cramps, which led him and MacKaye to unearth the punk-rockabilly band’s 1977 shelved album, produced by Alex Chilton. “Cool Evidence” prominently features the Cramps, via a busted drumhead, a handwritten sign for the group’s show at the Marquee in London, and assorted posters.

All of the artifacts that adorn the walls are encased in plexi-archival framing, which, according to Rollins, will last 100 years. But the framing, done locally, wasn’t cheap: Rollins estimates the whole endeavor will cost him six figures. “[The framers] see me and they’re like, ‘Oh my God, turkey dinner,'” he jokes. “But it feels vulgar to talk about money.”

The exhibit includes distinct sections that pay tribute to Rollins’ own history with punk, namely Black Flag, which he joined in 1981. There’s a photo of the band when they were called Panic and featured Keith Morris on vocals, along with a few original Raymond Pettibon drawings. But it’s the Stooges section that’s the most fascinating, thanks to Rollins’ status as the preeminent collector of the band’s items. “There’s hardly anything you can get now because no Stooges fan gets anything. It’s from my cold dead hands,” he says. Related Content How Dropkick Murphys Became Punk’s Loudest Anti-Trump Voice Haywire Are Living Their Hardcore Dreams Punk Rock Is Heating Up Across the Caribbean X-Ray Spex, the Raincoats, the Slits Members Form Supergroup for London Punk Festival

In the exhibit, Polaroids and other items obtained from Michael Tipton — who recorded the Stooges’ 1976 live album Metallic K.O., the masters of which Rollins now owns and wants to re-release — are on display. There’s an extremely rare photo of Pop putting on makeup before a 1968 show in Ann Arbor, Michigan, the only time the Stooges singer did so. Out-of-focus images of the band from Tipton in the early Seventies are also on display. “Who gets backstage with the Stooges while Iggy’s getting his hair together with an Aquanet?” Rollins says.

Seeing the exhibit come to life, and as organized as it is, is exactly what Rollins dreamed up while running on a gym treadmill years ago. “I’ve been thinking of this for years, and now I get to see what my idea looks like in the physical world,” he says. “It’ll take me probably another three years to figure out if this will work or not. I think the ingredients are awesome.”

As for what’s next, Rollins says he’s done performing music. “I’m not going out to do the first album again. I’ll have a heart attack,” he says. “Four wheezing men onstage – it’s pathetic.” He has a book coming out next March, which will take up most of 2027, and he may start teaching a class at Vanderbilt University. Trending Stories Paul McCartney and Ringo Starr to Reunite at Las Vegas Show in 2027 Watch Ariana Grande, Ben Stiller, Robert De Niro Take Over ‘The Tonight Show’ Pink Becomes First Solo Female Pop Artist to Headline the Sphere Judge Sets Steven Tyler Sex Assault Trial Date

Most importantly, however, he’s optimistic about taking “Cool Evidence” on tour. Honoring the history of punk has become something of a calling for Rollins.

“Fuck it, this is what I like to do,” he says. “I’m 65. I’m in the fourth quarter. I want to be a museum guy, an archive guy, a ‘give you your stuff’ guy, and my two primary fields of study: Stooges and the Cramps. I like reinvention — I’ve always benefited when I’ve stopped doing one thing.”

The Hot 100 flows through social media, so Billboard is bringing YouTube data back into its charts

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Billboard and YouTube have kissed and made up. A disagreement between the two companies kept the latter out of the former’s chart calculus for about a year, but Billboard is adjusting its weights to give more recognition to ad-supported streams that occur on YouTube. As a result, YouTube data will be incorporated into the Billboard charts (again) in 2027 and beyond.

Lyor Cohen, YouTube’s Global Head of Music, announced the new policy in a blog post. “We are returning to the Billboard charts starting in 2027,” Cohen wrote. “Billboard’s new methodology better reflects how fans engage with music today across both subscription and ad-supported services.”

YouTube views first became part of Billboard‘s rankings in 2013, when the music magazine recognized the growing influence of digital streams across its charts. As soon as the single-based Hot 100 and album-based 200 began factoring in YouTube-based plays, the platform’s impact became abundantly clear. At the time, Baauer’s “Harlem Shake” was the soundtrack to an inescapable viral trend, and the EDM banger shot to the top of the Hot 200 as a result.

Daily industry updates, straight to your inbox.

Soon, the Hot 100 was filled with songs that were big on YouTube, and that led detractors to argue that Billboard was overemphasizing ad-supported streams. Record companies and fans could use repetitive videos to push their favorite songs to the top of the charts — and that’s exactly what they did.

In a 2017 adjustment to its chart policies, Billboard decided to weigh paid streams more heavily than ad-supported streams. As of 2025, it took 2.5 streams on an ad-supported service like YouTube to equal one stream on a subscription-based service like Spotify Premium.

Cohen protested that policy, and the squabbles eventually compelled YouTube to pull its data from the Billboard charts. “Every play should count equally,” Cohen wrote at the time.

Notably, Billboard‘s updated policy does not count all streams equally. Paid streams are still given more weight, though the gap has shrunk. Now, one paid stream will equal 1.5 ad-supported streams. That breakdown, according to Billboard, “better reflects the varied user activity occurring on these services.”

Billboard wants to do right by all its partners, but its charts couldn’t ignore YouTube forever. The platform’s impact on music consumption is undeniable, even if some of its streams only last for a few seconds. So when the Billboard charts come out on January 16, they will better reflect the pop culture landscape as it currently looks.

Fairgame$ canceled

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Sony Interactive Entertainment has canceled development subsidiary Haven Interactive Studios‘ May 2023-announced multiplayer shooter Fairgame$, according to a Bloomberg report.

A spokesperson for Sony Interactive Entertainment told Bloomberg there will be no job cuts as a result of the cancellation, and that Haven Interactive Studios will move to support other studios within the organization.

In an email to employees today, which was reviewed by Bloomberg, Sony Interactive Entertainment executives Sean Decker and John Doyle said a recent playtest with thousands of players—likely the “Project Espresso” playtest we reported on in September—did not deliver on the results the organizations expected.

“While the test demonstrated strong operational excellence, live ops readiness, and community engagement, player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set,” the email reads. “Combined with challenging market conditions in the [player-versus-player] shooter genre, we have made the difficult decision to cease development on the game.”

Fairgame$ was planned for release on PlayStation 5 and PC.

Remodeling Activity Holds Flat in Q2 Amid Housing Slowdown

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The National Association of Home Builders (NAHB) reported that remodeling activity was flat in the second quarter amid a broader nationwide housing slowdown, in its latest State Projections of Remodeling (SPR).

The SPR showed a near-even split in the second quarter: Remodeling spending declined in 26 states and increased in 24 states, according to a release. The average growth rate across all states broke even at 0.0%.

“These flat results are consistent with NAHB’s forecast for remodeling spending in 2026,” NAHB Chief Economist Robert Dietz said. “Second-quarter spending growth was concentrated in smaller states. Among the 10 states with the largest market shares, only North Carolina and Illinois recorded growth.”

“Rising construction costs and weak consumer sentiment are weighing on projects and keeping spending flat, but the remodeling sector has solid long-term growth potential,” Elliott Pike—NAHB Remodelers chairman—stated. “An aging housing stock, the mortgage rate lock-in effect, home equity gains and more home owners choosing to age in place will support future growth.”

Remodeler confidence has also held steady. The release noted that NAHB’s Remodeling Market Index (RMI)—which measures confidence in the sector—has remained above 60 for the past year.

California led the nation in remodeling spending in the second quarter of 2026, accounting for about 8% of the market. The five largest state markets by share and dollar volume were:

The five states with the largest dollar increases in remodeling spending in the second quarter were:

As reported by the release, NAHB expects remodeling to grow in the years ahead as rising home equity helps more home owners fund projects, including upgrades that allow them to age in place. Remodeling also accounts for a growing share of residential construction, both in the number of firms and total construction spending. At the start of 2025, there were 128,000 remodeling firms, up from 69,000 in 2000.

Elon Musk Is Pulling the Plug on Cybertrucks to Focus on Something New

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Tesla plans to wind down Cybertruck production next year as Elon Musk shifts attention to its Cybercab. Here's what the change means.

Tesla Cybertrucks have dominated headlines even before they hit the road. But now it appears their time in the automotive world spotlight might be coming to an end. The company’s CEO Elon Musk says Tesla will wind down Cybertruck production at its Texas factory to make room for the Cybercab, the company’s self-driving taxi.

Learn why the company is changing direction and what the news means for the Cybertruck.

Austin American-Statesman/Hearst Newspapers/Getty Images

In a Nov. 6 post on X, Musk wrote, “Tesla will be winding down production of the Cybertruck at Gigafactory Texas around the middle of next year to make room for increased Cybercab production.”

Tesla began delivering Cybertrucks in late 2023, but sales haven’t lived up to expectations. According to Kelley Blue Book estimates published by Cox Automotive, U.S. sales fell by nearly half in 2025. The truck has also faced criticism for its price, design, and quality issues, as well as several safety recalls.

Current owners can continue driving their Cybertrucks, and existing warranty terms still apply. Cybertruck lead engineer Wes Morrill also addressed concerns about future repairs. “Tesla will maintain spare parts and service will continue support,” he wrote on X on November 6. He didn’t specify how long that support would last.

For anyone still waiting on a Cybertruck, the news is less encouraging. In a Nov. 9 post on X, Musk wrote, “We will try to make as many as possible before shutting down the line in about 8 months, but will have to return deposits for a lot of people.”

Tesla hasn’t said which customers will receive their trucks or how it plans to handle the remaining orders.

The Cybercab is Tesla’s two-seat electric car designed to drive itself. It doesn’t have a steering wheel or pedals because it won’t need a human driver. Tesla has already started offering Cybercab rides in parts of Austin. The company plans to expand the service, although the vehicles aren’t widely available yet.

Musk denies that Tesla is ending production because the Cybertruck isn’t profitable. He says the company can put its Texas factory to better use by making Cybercabs.

Bella Hadid Hard Launches a New Icy Blond Hair Color

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

The supermodel brought a bit of extra frost to the stadium while attending an NHL game

Bella Hadid is no stranger to major hair changes—seamlessly going from chunky highlights to chestnut brown just this year—but her latest color debut may be her most shocking yet.

Hadid took to Instagram to share a series of photos from Saturday night’s Colorado Avalanche game, which honored Hockey Fights Cancer, a charitable organization through the National Hockey League, and among the ice and jerseys was the supermodel’s fresh icy-blond hair, which stood out in a sea of team colors.

Even though the model wore a Colorado Avalanche hat, her bright bleach-blond color and blunt shoulder-length bob peeked through. Hadid’s hair has skewed more blond lately, but this new color is icier and brighter, and it’s shorter, which suggests the debut look may be a wig disguise.

If Hadid did take the plunge and dye her hair, it’s likely by New York–based celebrity colorist Jenna Perry, who has colored Hadid’s hair in the past and is responsible for Hadid’s blond and brunette colors, plus other celebrity colors like Kendall Jenner’s red and several of Kaia Gerber’s blond and brunette variations.

Hadid isn’t the only celebrity to debut a new bright blond color this week. As a departure from his classic brunette hair, Jacob Elordi was recently spotted with fresh blond hair peeking out of a beanie hat while with girlfriend Kendall Jenner at a Broadway show, according to photos shared on the social media account Deux Moi.

Is bleach blond the new color for fall, or are Hadid and Elordi’s new hues just a phase?

According to a release, SERHANT. Utah opens with more than 40 agents from firms including Berkshire Hathaway HomeServices, Engel & Völkers, Real, Sotheby’s International Realty, and Windermere Real Estate, who together have closed $700 million in sales over the past 12 months. Utah marks the firm’s sixth new market across the U.S. in 2026, following launches in Massachusetts, California, Texas, Colorado and Illinois earlier this year, and brings SERHANT.’s national footprint to 21 states.

Every SERHANT. agent has access to S.MPLE, the firm’s proprietary platform, which puts AI-powered tools for lead generation, marketing and transaction management in agents’ hands.

She started her career in commercial real estate in Arizona before returning home. At SERHANT. Utah, she leads agent recruitment, mentorship, and brokerage operations, building a culture designed to help agents grow sustainable, high-performing businesses.

“When I looked at what SERHANT. has built, nothing else compared,” said Osguthorpe. “The tools and resources they give agents for their day-to-day business are best in class, and they pair that with genuine care for and connection with their people. That combination sets SERHANT. apart.”

Named one of the Top 100 Sotheby’s International Realty advisors worldwide in 2025, Snyder closed more than $105 million in volume last year. That total includes representing the buyer of a legacy property in Park City’s Old Ranch Road area, listed at $25 million and purchased for an undisclosed record-breaking price. Snyder has worked in real estate for a decade and represents buyers and sellers across Park City, Deer Valley, and the Wasatch Back, on everything from fractional ownership to luxury estates, land, and investment properties. She graduated magna cum laude from Cornell University and built retail businesses before real estate. Active within her community, Julie donates a portion of every transaction to local organizations supporting winter sports, Park City’s trail system, and animal rescue, including U.S. Ski & Snowboard, Mountain Trails Foundation, and Nuzzles & Co.

“Becoming a founder at SERHANT. Utah was about finding a company that better matches the way I think about real estate,” said Snyder. “SERHANT. has built a company around marketing, media, technology, creativity, and innovation in a way that feels aligned with where this industry is headed. I’m excited to bring those tools to my clients while continuing to deliver the same local knowledge, strategy, and personal service that have always been at the center of my business.”

Oxford University Museum Returns 39 Sets of Ancestral Remains to Naga People of India

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

At a ceremony held on November 9, the Pitt Rivers Museum at Oxford University in the UK formally returned 39 sets of ancestral remains to the Naga people of India. The remains, primarily human skulls, were taken during the British colonial rule of India and Burma (now Myanmar) and have been in the museum’s collection for more than a century. A delegation of almost 30 community members from Nagaland, an Indian state bordering Myanmar, traveled to Britain for the handover.

The Pitt Rivers claims to hold the largest collection of Naga artifacts in the world, most of which were acquired by John Henry Hutton, an anthropologist and colonial administrator in the Indian Civil Service, and a second administrator/anthropologist, James Philip Mills, between 1910 and 1940. Within that collection, 219 items are listed as Naga human remains, including 39 ancestral human remains and 178 objects that contain, or may contain, human hair. (The current return concerns only the 39 ancestors.) Related Articles Sweden Returns Benin Bronze Trove to Nigeria, Indigenous Remains to Bolivia Museum Wiesbaden Will Be Germany's First Institution to Return Colonial-Era Human Remains to Indonesia

On its website, the museum notes that colonial “collecting” was “often bound up with the structures of colonial violence; remains and objects were frequently confiscated during punitive military expeditions, rather than freely given.” Mills, who wrote three monographs on Naga culture and practice, refers to at least one armed expedition to demand “headhunting trophies” from Pangsha village, remarking that the Pitt Rivers would thus “get some fine specimens.”

While the Naga people practiced headhunting at the time, taking the severed heads of enemies as trophies, they also kept and cared for the heads of their own chiefs. According to anthropologist Dolly Kikon (a member of Nagaland’s Lotha-Naga community, now at the University of California, Santa Cruz) one of the remains being returned is a chief’s skull.

The Pitt Rivers removed all human remains, including those of Naga people, from display in 2020. Sensing an opening, Kikon contacted the museum’s director, Laura Van Broekhoven and offered to connect her with the Naga community. The return is the culmination of the museum’s work with Naga partners, including the Forum for Naga Reconciliation (FNR) together with tribal Hohos, elders, churches and communities.

Naga tribal leaders plan to bury the remains together in a joint memorial park. “They’ve been here in a foreign land for more than a century,” said Dr Vihuto Asumi, President of the Sumî Hoho Naga tribe. “We believe that their spirits are still not at rest, so by doing this, we believe it will bring spiritual healing, not only to the ancestral remains here but to everyone who died during the headhunting days.”

Cucurella defends Vinicius Jr over Diomande comparison as Real Madrid pause eight-game winning run

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

By Alexis Pereira | 10 Nov 2026 20:52

Marc Cucurella has defended Vinicius Junior amid the Brazilian’s difficult start to the season, insisting that team-mate Yan Diomande does not help him more defensively than the Real Madrid forward does. Real Madrid have paused an eight-game winning run for the international break, during the best spell under boss Jose Mourinho, and are hoping Vinicius rediscovers his form with Brazil under Carlo Ancelotti. Cucurella shares that wish.

Speaking on the Cope radio programme El Partidazo, the Spanish left-back was asked about the poor start to the season of Vinicius, who has just one goal and three assists in 12 matches. The decline in his form, together with a suspension that kept him out of the last four La Liga games, has led to the meteoric rise of Diomande, who has made a difference on the left side.

Beyond four goals and four assists in 16 matches, the Ivory Coast forward has been praised for his tactical commitment, helping Cucurella with defensive recovery. But the Real Madrid number 17 rejects the idea that Diomande helps him more with marking than the Brazilian does.

‘I do not think he helps me more, I think he is more visible,’ Cucurella said. ‘Diomande runs back and is everywhere. Vinicius goes back three times and then, the fourth time he does not run, the ball is played in. If Vini scored three goals per game, nobody would say anything to him.’

Days after his signing from Chelsea was announced in late June, the defender told Radio Marca that he ‘did not mind’ if Vinicius ‘does not track back or help in defence’, because he wanted the number seven energised to have an impact in the final third with goals. Cucurella also told Vinicius that he would do all the ‘dirty work’ for him.

Despite that consent, the forward has helped with the compactness without the ball, following the Portuguese boss’s guidance. The main problem is that, in possession, the 26-year-old has not shown the same efficiency as in recent years in dribbles in one-on-one situations and in decision-making.

Like other figures at Real Madrid, the Spaniard came out in defence of Vinicius, considering a dip in form natural after consecutive seasons among the best players in the world. The left-back also insisted that Vinicius has been working behind the scenes to react and regain his prominence, which he considers fundamental to Mourinho’s success in 2026-27.

‘Vinicius is a player who has been performing at a spectacular level for five years, so it is understandable that he is going through a difficult phase now,’ Cucurella continued. ‘I see that he is motivated and works hard in training, and that is what matters.’

‘The most important thing for us is that he returns to his best level, because if he is at his peak, we have a great team,’ he concluded.

OpenAI, Anthropic face new oversight as New York’s AI safety law takes effect

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

New York is gearing up to enforce one of the country’s most significant AI safety laws. The state opened a registration portal Tuesday, with which large frontier AI developers like Anthropic and OpenAI will be required to register by January 1, 2027.

The RAISE Act, New York’s safety and transparency bill, kicks in at the start of the year. It mandates that large frontier developers — companies with more than $500 million in annual revenue — have to publish safety frameworks on their websites, report critical safety incidents within 72 hours, and issue public model transparency reports.

The newly formed Office of Digital Innovation, Governance, Integrity, and Trust (DIGIT) will implement the RAISE Act and oversee large frontier AI labs. Penalties can be as high as $1 million for one violation and $3 million for subsequent violations.

The state also can bring enforcement actions against companies that fail to register with the new portal, though a spokesperson declined to provide specifics.

“Tech moguls tell us that their vision for AI is inevitable,” Governor Kathy Hochul said Tuesday during a press briefing. “They tell us get on board or get left behind… That any collateral damage is worth it as long as we win the race to the AI cliff.”

On Tuesday, Hochul also launched the New York Center for AI Responsibility and Research based at State University of New York’s Binghamton University. The center will independently evaluate the safety, security, and operation of advanced AI systems and work with DIGIT to review companies’ safety frameworks. Hochul first announced the center in January with $55 million in donations and capital.

“We’re building a national model for smart, sensible AI regulation, a model that puts communities above corporations and people above powerful technology,” Hochul said.

The RAISE Act isn’t the only safety law New York has passed. Last year, the state began enforcing a law that requires AI companion operators to regularly let users know they’re talking to a bot, and to make reasonable efforts to detect suicidal ideation and self-harm.

In October, Hochul signed a separate chatbot safety law for minors, restricting behaviors like deceptive human-like interactions, emotionally manipulative or sycophantic responses, and encouragement of behaviors like suicide or disordered eating. That law takes effect June 1, 2027.

Other states have also passed similar AI safety and transparency bills to the RAISE Act. California’s SB 53, which took effect in January, mandates that frontier AI labs have in place and publish safety frameworks, enact whistleblower protections, and report critical safety incidents within 15 days. Illinois’ SB 315, which will go into effect at the start of 2027, has similar requirements, plus includes independent safety compliance audits.

Niclas Füllkrug credits Werder Bremen medical staff for helping him rediscover form

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Germany international Niclas Füllkrug has reflected on his return to Werder Bremen and explained why his familiar surroundings have helped him rediscover his best form following two challenging years abroad. The 33-year-old striker has enjoyed an outstanding start to the 2026/27 Bundesliga campaign, scoring nine league goals since returning to Werder Bremen from West Ham United during the summer transfer window.

Füllkrug’s performances have also earned him a recall to the German national team under Jürgen Klopp, bringing an end to an absence of more than a year. Speaking at Tuesday’s DFB press conference in Herzogenaurach, Füllkrug was asked to reflect on his experiences in England and Italy, where injuries and inconsistent playing time prevented him from establishing himself at West Ham and AC Milan.

The veteran centre-forward nevertheless rejected the suggestion that his time abroad had been entirely unsuccessful, emphasising the importance of his family and the positive experiences he enjoyed away from football. The particular adjective Füllkrug took issue with was “terrible”.

“Let me first emphasize that there was never a time in my life during which things were going ‘terrible’,” Füllkrug said in response to a question about his disappointing spell in England. “There are many components to life. Football is important, but my daughter and my family are central. I was able to take a lot from those years and build some fond memories with my family.”

“I simply couldn’t keep fit,” Füllkrug continued. “That’s all. I didn’t forget anything during these past two years, and I believe I’ve proven that now. I think the key was to be healthy and be on a team that passes to you in the crucial moments. Full praise to my teammates at Werder. I benefit enormously from the way the lads are playing the ball to me.”

Füllkrug’s return to Bremen has already proven to be one of the Bundesliga’s most successful summer moves. The striker quickly reestablished himself as a central figure in Werder’s attack, combining his goalscoring instincts with strong hold-up play, aerial ability, and an increasingly important role in the team’s attacking combinations.

His contributions have been particularly significant for Daniel Thioune’s side, with Füllkrug emerging as one of the Bundesliga’s leading goalscorers during the opening months of the campaign. Beyond the tactical and sporting considerations, however, Füllkrug believes that his longstanding personal connection to Werder has played a decisive role in helping him settle back into Bundesliga football.

“Werder is simply my home,” Füllkrug said when pressed further about how his performances had improved in a Werder shirt. “It’s my footballing home. As you all know, one sleeps the best in one’s own bed. I immediately felt comfortable there. I joined the academy at the age of 14 and, in my estimation, I believe about 70-90 percent of the same people are on staff there.”

“I’m healthy and there’s a medical department there that takes good care of me,” Füllkrug continued. “They’ve managed to get me back on track and ensure that I haven’t missed any training sessions. And we have a great team from which I benefit. The transfers that we brought in this summer have all done great. I just happen to be one of them.”

Remember PS5-exclusive shooter Fairgame$? It’s reportedly canceled now, to no one’s surprise

📅 2026-11-10  |  Read Source →

Well, it seems like this one was only a matter of time.

Sony has reportedly canceled the long-missing online shooter Fairgame$ after it went missing in the public eye for years, according to Bloomberg’s Jason Schreier, adding to a long list of canceled PlayStation projects this generation.

Schreier said that “a recent test of the game with thousands of players did not deliver on the results PlayStation expected,” according to an internal email sent to employees today.

First revealed at PlayStation Showcase way back in May 2023, the game seemed to be themed around online heist PvP gameplay. But it hasn’t been seen since, and this cancellation comes as little surprise, as rumors about turmoil in development have been swirling for some time.

“While the test demonstrated strong operational excellence, live ops readiness and community engagement, player feedback and retention results were mixed and fell short of the goals we had set,” executives Sean Decker and John Doyle said in the memo. “Combined with challenging market conditions in the PVP shooter genre, we have made the difficult decision to cease development on the game.”

Earlier this year, it was reported that Fairgame$ was pivoting away from the “heist” mechanic and instead focusing on the extraction game type, similar to Bungie’s Marathon and Call of Duty’s DMZ. It apparently did not work out.

Fairgame$, developed by Haven Studios, joins a long list of PS5 projects that did not make the cut this generation as Sony incessantly chased live-service success. Concord launched and was quickly shuttered, while The Last of Us Factions, a God of War game, a Spider-Man game, Twisted Metal, and more never saw the light of day from PS5’s games-as-a-service initiative.

According to Schreier, “there will be no job cuts as a result of the cancellation,” and “Haven will instead move to support other studios within the organization.”
